Completion and task workio_uring/io_uring.c, io_uring/tw.cio_fill_cqe_req() posts the CQE; the task-work machinery in tw.c is how a completion raised in interrupt context gets back to the submitting thread. Follow io_req_task_work_add() to see why IORING_SETUP_COOP_TASKRUN and IORING_SETUP_DEFER_TASKRUN exist: they are different answers to how eagerly that deferred work should be run.
The operation tableio_uring/opdef.c, io_uring/opdef.hOne entry per opcode, and the best map of the subsystem there is. Each io_issue_def carries the prep and issue handlers plus the one-bit properties that tell the core how the operation behaves — whether it needs a file, whether it can poll, whether it must run unbounded. Adding an operation means adding a row here, which is why the opcode set has grown so easily.
Ordering: links, drains, and timeoutsio_uring/timeout.c, io_uring/io_uring.cio_queue_next() and io_fail_links() implement the chain semantics, including the rule that a failure cancels the rest of a soft link. timeout.c holds both the standalone timeout and the linked timeout that arms a deadline on its predecessor.
The async worker poolio_uring/io-wq.c, io_uring/sqpoll.cio-wq.c is a self-contained work-queue implementation worth reading in its own right: bounded and unbounded workers, per-process accounting, and the hashing that serializes buffered writes to one inode. sqpoll.c is the other way to avoid a system call, and the contrast between them is the chapter's point — one removes the submission call, the other absorbs blocking work.
Registered files and buffersio_uring/rsrc.c, io_uring/filetable.cWhere the per-operation costs are paid once instead of every time. rsrc.c holds the pinned-page bookkeeping for registered buffers; filetable.c is the fixed-file table that also makes direct descriptors possible — file references that exist only inside a ring and never occupy a slot in the process's descriptor table.
Provided buffersio_uring/kbuf.cThe answer to a problem specific to receiving: a server posting many concurrent receives does not know which will complete first. Read the IORING_REGISTER_PBUF_RING path, the shared ring the application refills without a system call, and note that the chosen buffer comes back in the CQE flags rather than in the SQE.
Multi-shot and pollio_uring/poll.c, io_uring/net.cpoll.c is the internal poll machinery that lets an operation wait for readiness without occupying a worker, and it is also the basis of multi-shot. Look for IORING_CQE_F_MORE: it is the single bit that turns one SQE into a stream of completions, and net.c is where accept and receive use it.
Zero-copy and passthroughio_uring/notif.c, io_uring/uring_cmd.c, io_uring/zcrx.cnotif.c implements the second, notification CQE that tells a zero-copy send when its pages may be reused — the detail that makes the feature easy to misuse. uring_cmd.c is the open-ended one: a device-specific command carried over the rings, which is how NVMe passthrough works. zcrx.c is the receive-side counterpart.
Who may use io_uringio_uring/io_uring.c, security/security.cThe io_uring_disabled sysctl and its three values, and the security_uring_allowed() hook that still has the final say even when the sysctl permits. The companion hooks security_uring_sqpoll(), security_uring_cmd() and security_uring_override_creds() are how a security module can allow io_uring while refusing its riskier parts.
Restrictions and BPF filtersio_uring/register.c, io_uring/bpf_filter.cHow a program confines itself rather than being confined. io_register_restrictions() in register.c only works on a ring created with IORING_SETUP_R_DISABLED, which forces the safe order: register resources, narrow the ring, then enable it. bpf_filter.c is the newer and more interesting half — a per-opcode filter written in classic BPF, installed through the same bpf_prog_create_from_user() that seccomp uses, and gated on no_new_privs for the same reason. Note the blind registration path near the end of register.c, where a file descriptor of -1 attaches restrictions to the task instead of to one ring.
Messaging, futexes, and cancellationio_uring/msg_ring.c, io_uring/futex.c, io_uring/cancel.cThe operations that act on the ring machinery rather than on a file. msg_ring.c posts a completion into another ring's queue, or moves a registered descriptor between rings. futex.c folds futex waiting (Chapter 19) into the completion loop. cancel.c is the one every real server needs, and its flags — matching by file descriptor, by opcode, or everything — are what make it usable when a connection drops.
Watching a live ringio_uring/fdinfo.cA shortcut for understanding: /proc/<pid>/fdinfo/<fd> on an io_uring descriptor prints the head and tail of both rings, the registered files and buffers, and the state of the SQPOLL thread. Reading it is the quickest way to see the structures of this chapter with real values in them.
Native AIO, the interface io_uring replacedfs/aio.c, include/uapi/linux/aio_abi.hThe io_setup/io_submit family behind libaio. Read it to see what io_uring was reacting to — and note that POSIX AIO (aio_read) is not here at all, because glibc emulates it with threads.
Non-blocking issue and the punt to a workerio_uring/rw.c (io_read, io_write), include/linux/fs.h (IOCB_NOWAIT)How buffered I/O is made asynchronous: try it with IOCB_NOWAIT in the submitting task, and on -EAGAIN arm a poll wait or hand it to io-wq. The heart of §1.1.
The ring ABI and its setup flagsinclude/uapi/linux/io_uring.h, io_uring/register.c (io_register_resize_rings)The stable contract: io_uring_params, the 21 IORING_SETUP_* flags, the 18 IORING_FEAT_* bits, and the IORING_OFF_* mmap offsets. The newest flags (SQE_MIXED, CQE_MIXED, SQ_REWIND) show the layout still moving in 7.0.
The two operation tablesio_uring/opdef.c, io_uring/opdef.hio_issue_defs holds the hot fields (prep, issue, and fourteen property bits); io_cold_defs holds the name and the teardown callbacks. The split exists to keep the common path cache-dense.
Waiting for completionsio_uring/wait.c, io_uring/tw.cThe two-stage wait built on min_wait_usec, the hrtimer that switches from batch mode to first-completion mode, and the task-work running that finalizes completions in the owner's context.
Asynchronous process reapingio_uring/waitid.cTurns waitid into a ring operation, so a supervisor need not dedicate a thread or a signal handler to noticing that a child changed state.
Draining as a two-sided barrierio_uring/io_uring.c (io_init_drain, io_drain_req)Shows that IOSQE_IO_DRAIN blocks in both directions: drain_active keeps deferring later submissions onto defer_list until the barrier clears.
What an io_uring thread really iskernel/fork.c (create_io_thread)Both the SQPOLL thread and the io-wq workers are clones of the submitting task with CLONE_VM, CLONE_FILES, and CLONE_THREAD, marked PF_IO_WORKER and never returning to user mode. This is why work they issue carries the owner's context.
Bounded, unbounded, and hashed workio_uring/io_uring.c (io_prep_async_work), io_uring/io-wq.cWhere unbound_nonreg_file chooses the worker account and hash_reg_file stamps a request with its inode hash so io-wq runs only one such item at a time.
Incremental buffer consumptionio_uring/kbuf.c, include/uapi/linux/io_uring.hA group registered with IOU_PBUF_RING_INC fills one buffer across several completions; IORING_CQE_F_BUF_MORE marks a buffer as partly used and due to reappear.
Direct descriptors and their slotsio_uring/filetable.c, io_uring/rsrc.cIORING_FILE_INDEX_ALLOC, the range restriction imposed by IORING_REGISTER_FILE_ALLOC_RANGE, and the cloning of a pinned buffer table between rings.
Hybrid polling for storageio_uring/rw.c (io_uring_hybrid_poll)Sleeps for a learned estimate of device latency before busy-polling, keeping the smallest observed runtime so a ring spanning mixed devices does not oversleep the fastest.
Zero-copy that may not beio_uring/net.c, io_uring/notif.cThe notification CQE, and IORING_SEND_ZC_REPORT_USAGE, which sets IORING_NOTIF_USAGE_ZC_COPIED when the stack copied after all.
Task-scoped confinementio_uring/register.c (io_uring_register_blind), kernel/fork.c (io_uring_fork)Registering restrictions or a BPF filter with a descriptor of -1 attaches them to the task at current->io_uring_restrict; they are cloned into every ring the task later creates and into every child it forks.
Fairness across rings on one poll threadio_uring/sqpoll.c (__io_sq_thread, IORING_SQPOLL_CAP_ENTRIES_VALUE)When one SQPOLL thread serves several rings, each pass drains at most eight SQEs from any single ring before moving to the next, so a busy ring cannot starve its neighbors.
The poll thread's own task-work and NAPI pollingio_uring/sqpoll.c (io_sq_tw), io_uring/napi.c (io_napi_sqpoll_busy_poll)Each pass of the SQPOLL loop also runs the thread's pending task work and, for rings with busy-poll receive configured, polls their NAPI IDs — the one thread ends up driving submission, completion callbacks, and receive-side polling together.
io-wq is per-task, and SQPOLL rings redirect to itio_uring/tctx.c, io_uring/register.c (io_register_iowq_max_workers), io_uring/sqpoll.c (io_sqpoll_wq_cpu_affinity)A request's tctx is set from current->io_uring at submission time, so on an SQPOLL ring — where submission happens inside the poll thread — blocking work runs through the poll thread's own worker pool; registering worker limits or CPU affinity on such a ring is redirected there transparently.
Registered buffers are charged per user, not per processio_uring/rsrc.c (__io_account_mem)RLIMIT_MEMLOCK for pinned registered buffers is tracked in user->locked_vm, keyed by the real UID that created the ring, so every process running as that user draws on one shared budget; a second counter on the owning process's mm_struct exists only so /proc/pid/status reports it.
Cloning registered buffers between ringsio_uring/rsrc.c (io_clone_buffers)IORING_REGISTER_CLONE_BUFFERS shares pinned pages between rings by duplicating resource nodes rather than re-pinning; IORING_REGISTER_SRC_REGISTERED and IORING_REGISTER_DST_REPLACE control how the source is named and whether existing destination entries may be overwritten.
Retiring a provided buffer before it runs dryio_uring/kbuf.c (io_kbuf_inc_commit)An incrementally consumed buffer's addr and len are updated in place after each completion; a registration's min_left floor makes the kernel move on to the next buffer rather than offer a remainder too small to be useful.
Multi-shot's completion protocol is one mechanism, many opcodesio_uring/rw.c, io_uring/net.c, io_uring/timeout.c, io_uring/uring_cmd.c, io_uring/zcrx.c (all set IORING_CQE_F_MORE)READ_MULTISHOT, the recurring timeout, multi-shot command passthrough, and zero-copy receive all re-arm through the same flag as multi-shot accept, poll, and recv, rather than each having its own protocol.
Multi-shot's extra completions bypass the overflow listio_uring/io_uring.c (io_req_post_cqe, io_fill_cqe_aux)Unlike an ordinary completion, a multi-shot request's per-occurrence CQE is not queued to the overflow list when the ring is full; posting simply fails, and the result is instead delivered as the stream's final, overflow-protected completion while the multi-shot flag is left clear.
An IOPOLL ring rejects unsupported opcodes at submissionio_uring/io_uring.c (io_init_req, the def->iopoll check)An operation whose op-table entry does not advertise polled completion is rejected with -EINVAL when submitted on an IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL ring, rather than silently falling back to interrupt-driven completion.
Zero-copy send without registration still touches RLIMIT_MEMLOCKio_uring/notif.h (io_notif_account_mem)A zero-copy send that does not name a registered buffer still pins its pages for the life of the notification and charges them against the same per-user RLIMIT_MEMLOCK budget as registered buffers; naming a registered buffer skips this because those pages are already accounted.
Attached filters compose by conjunction, never relaxationio_uring/bpf_filter.c (__io_uring_run_bpf_filters, io_register_bpf_filter)A newly registered filter is chained ahead of any filter already attached to an opcode, but the check requires every filter in the chain to return one; a later registration can only narrow what an opcode permits, never restore permission an earlier filter withheld.
A filter that would rather fail than guessio_uring/bpf_filter.c (IO_URING_BPF_FILTER_SZ_STRICT)An opcode's filter context struct can grow new fields across kernel versions; by default a filter written for an older, smaller pdu_size just runs against its own layout, but this flag turns the size mismatch into an outright -EMSGSIZE at registration time instead.
The BPF filter mechanism is itself optionalio_uring/Kconfig (CONFIG_IO_URING_BPF)Depends on CONFIG_BPF and CONFIG_NET; a kernel built without networking or the BPF subsystem falls back to the coarser opcode and registration-op allow-lists for per-application containment.
Where a forked task's restrictions are actually copiedio_uring/tctx.c (__io_uring_fork), include/linux/io_uring.h (io_uring_fork)kernel/fork.c's copy_process calls the inline wrapper io_uring_fork, which allocates a private copy and calls __io_uring_fork to duplicate the restriction set into it — the actual cloning logic lives in io_uring's own tctx code, not in the fork path itself.

Chapter 25 — Containers, Namespaces, and Cgroups

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
The nsproxy and the eight namespace typesinclude/linux/nsproxy.h, kernel/nsproxy.c, include/linux/ns/ns_common_types.h, include/linux/proc_ns.hstruct nsproxy holds pointers for six of the eight types (UTS, IPC, mount, network, time, cgroup) plus the pid_ns_for_children and time_ns_for_children slots that take effect only for the next child; the user namespace lives in struct cred, and the active PID namespace is derived from struct pid. create_new_namespaces() is where every CLONE_NEW* flag except CLONE_NEWUSER is turned into a fresh proxy, and switch_task_namespaces() shows why nsproxy is refcounted and replaced rather than edited in place — threads that share all namespaces share one proxy. Every namespace embeds a struct ns_common whose proc_ns_operations vtable (get, put, install, owner, get_parent) lets generic code serve all eight types.
clone, unshare, and setnskernel/fork.c (copy_namespaces via copy_process, ksys_unshare), kernel/nsproxy.c (SYSCALL_DEFINE2(setns, ...), prepare_nsset, validate_nsset, commit_nsset)Three different ways to end up with a new set of namespaces: at creation time with a CLONE_NEW* flag, after the fact by unsharing the caller’s own namespaces, or by joining an already-existing one via a /proc/pid/ns/* file descriptor or a pidfd. setns() is the strictest of the three: it requires CAP_SYS_ADMIN in the user namespace that owns the target, and the prepare/validate/commit split makes a multi-namespace join all-or-nothing, entering the user namespace first because it authorizes the rest. copy_namespaces() returning early when no CLONE_NEW* flag is set is why namespaces cost an ordinary fork only a reference increment.
The nsfs filesystem, namespace lifetime, and listnsfs/nsfs.c, kernel/nscommon.c, kernel/nstree.c (SYSCALL_DEFINE4(listns, ...), ns_tree_add)Every namespace is really a file: an inode on the internal nsfs mount, referenced from /proc/pid/ns/*. Holding that file descriptor open, or bind-mounting it as ip netns add does, keeps the namespace alive with no member process; the NS_GET_* ioctls in nsfs.c walk ownership and nesting and return the stable ns_id. nscommon.c holds the split between the memory reference count and the active reference count that decides whether a namespace can still be listed or reopened, and nstree.c keeps the per-type trees that listns() walks; may_see_all_namespaces() is the check that reserves host-wide enumeration for real root in the initial namespaces.
UID/GID mapping, fake root, and idmapped mountskernel/user_namespace.c (proc_uid_map_write, new_idmap_permitted, verify_root_map, map_id_range_down), fs/mnt_idmapping.c, Documentation/admin-guide/namespaces/resource-control.rstproc_uid_map_write() is where /proc/pid/uid_map is parsed and validated — the single write-once step that turns an unprivileged process into an apparent root inside its own user namespace without granting it anything outside. new_idmap_permitted() enforces the one-line self-map limit for a writer without CAP_SETUID over the parent and the setgroups “deny” precondition for gid_map; verify_root_map() refuses to map inside-root unless the creator held CAP_SETFCAP. mnt_idmapping.c carries the same translation onto a single mount (struct mnt_idmap, vfsuid_t).
Capabilities are per user-namespacesecurity/commoncap.c (cap_capable, cap_capable_helper), kernel/user_namespace.c (set_cred_user_ns), kernel/capability.c (ns_capable)cap_capable() walks up the user-namespace parent chain: a capability held in a namespace is privilege only inside that namespace and the namespaces nested under it, never in its parent or any other ancestor. The one upward rule is visible in cap_capable_helper(): the creator’s effective UID in the parent namespace (ns->owner) is treated as holding every capability in the child. set_cred_user_ns() shows the new namespace’s full capability set and the cleared inheritable and ambient sets. This is the check that makes “root in a container” nowhere near as dangerous as real root, provided nothing else is misconfigured.
PID namespaces and struct pidinclude/linux/pid_namespace.h, kernel/pid.c (alloc_pid, register_pidns_sysctls), kernel/pid_namespace.cstruct pid’s numbers[] array holds one struct upid per namespace level the process is visible in, which is how the same task can be PID 4021 on the host and PID 1 inside its container. alloc_pid() allocates a number at every level against that level’s own pid_max, which register_pidns_sysctls() exposes per namespace. MAX_PID_NS_LEVEL (32) bounds the nesting depth, and zap_pid_ns_processes() in kernel/pid_namespace.c is the all-or-nothing teardown that runs when a namespace’s PID 1 dies.
Network, mount, and time namespaces; the new mount APInet/core/net_namespace.c (copy_net_ns), fs/namespace.c (copy_mnt_ns, open_tree, move_mount, mount_setattr, open_tree_attr), kernel/time/namespace.c (proc_timens_set_offset)copy_net_ns() and the per-subsystem pernet_operations it runs show why a network namespace is the heaviest type: every protocol registers its own per-namespace init and exit. copy_mnt_ns() copies the mount tree and, when the new namespace has a less privileged owner, locks the inherited mounts. The open_tree(OPEN_TREE_CLONE) / mount_setattr(MOUNT_ATTR_IDMAP) / move_mount() sequence is how a runtime builds a detached, fully configured mount tree and grafts it into place at once. proc_timens_set_offset() is the write side of /proc/pid/timens_offsets, accepted only before any task has entered the time namespace.
The unified cgroup v2 hierarchy, freeze, and killkernel/cgroup/cgroup.c (cgroup_mkdir, cgroup_kill, cgroup_post_fork), kernel/cgroup/freezer.c, kernel/cgroup/rstat.ccgroup_mkdir() shows the hierarchy is just a kernfs directory tree with controllers enabled per subtree via cgroup.subtree_control. rstat.c’s deferred, per-CPU statistic aggregation (flush-on-read rather than update-on-write) is the same combine-on-read idea PSI applies with its own per-CPU psi_group_cpu state. freezer.c implements cgroup.freeze by setting JOBCTL_TRAP_FREEZE on each task, which then parks itself in do_freezer_trap() (kernel/signal.c); cgroup_kill() bumps kill_seq so that cgroup_post_fork() can kill a child forked during the walk.
css, css_set, and task membershipinclude/linux/cgroup-defs.h, kernel/cgroup/cgroup.c (find_css_set, cgroup_migrate_prepare_dst, cgroup_migrate_execute)A task points at a css_set, not at a cgroup directly; find_css_set() deduplicates identical combinations of per-controller cgroup_subsys_state pointers so that every task with the same membership shares one css_set object instead of allocating a fresh one. cgroup_migrate_prepare_dst() allocates every destination set before cgroup_migrate_execute() moves anything, which is why a write to cgroup.procs either fully succeeds or leaves the task where it was.
The cpu, memory, and io controllerskernel/sched/core.c (cpu_cgrp_subsys), kernel/sched/fair.c (sched_group_set_shares, sched_group_set_idle), mm/memcontrol.c, block/blk-cgroup.c, block/blk-throttle.c, block/blk-iocost.c, block/blk-iolatency.cThree controllers with very different mechanisms. The cpu controller turns cpu.weight and cpu.idle into a task group’s weight and idle flag in the EEVDF fair class, and cpu.max into CFS bandwidth throttling. The memory controller (mm/memcontrol.c) is the largest single controller in the kernel: it charges pages and kernel objects as they are allocated and implements memory.max, memory.high throttling, the memory.min/memory.low protections, and memory.oom.group. The io controller’s core in blk-cgroup.c delegates to three policies: blk-throttle.c for io.max, blk-iocost.c for io.weight, and blk-iolatency.c for io.latency. (The old kernel/sched/cpuacct.c serves only cgroup v1.)
The pids, cpuset, and misc controllers, and delegationkernel/cgroup/pids.c, kernel/cgroup/cpuset.c (PRS_ROOT, PRS_ISOLATED), kernel/cgroup/misc.c, include/linux/misc_cgroup.h, kernel/cgroup/cgroup.c (nsdelegate, CGRP_ROOT_NS_DELEGATE)pids.c is one of the simplest controllers in the tree and a good first read; it caps the number of tasks, not memory or CPU. In cpuset.c, the PRS_ROOT and PRS_ISOLATED partition states are what cpuset.cpus.partition writes select. misc_cgroup.h lists the scarce resources the misc controller rations (SEV and SEV-ES ASIDs, TDX HKIDs). The nsdelegate mount option sets CGRP_ROOT_NS_DELEGATE, which makes a cgroup namespace root a delegation boundary so that an unprivileged container can create and manage its own sub-cgroups without host-side privilege.
The rdma, misc, and dmem device-resource controllerskernel/cgroup/rdma.c (rdmacg_try_charge), drivers/infiniband/core/cgroup.c, kernel/cgroup/misc.c (misc_cg_try_charge), kernel/cgroup/dmem.c (dmem_cgroup_try_charge, dmem_cgroup_state_evict_valuable), include/linux/cgroup_dmem.h, drivers/gpu/drm/drm_drv.c (drmm_cgroup_register_region), drivers/gpu/drm/ttm/ttm_resource.c (ttm_resource_alloc), drivers/gpu/drm/ttm/ttm_bo.c (ttm_bo_evict_cb, ttm_bo_evict_alloc)All three controllers only count. The driver charges before it allocates. dmem.c is short and reuses the memory controller's page_counter and its min/low protection calculation for device memory. Read dmem_cgroup_state_evict_valuable() beside TTM's two-pass eviction walk to see how an over-limit charge becomes eviction confined to the offending subtree.
Pressure Stall Informationkernel/sched/psi.c, include/linux/psi_types.h, kernel/cgroup/cgroup.c (cgroup_pressure_write)psi_task_change() walks the task's cgroup and every ancestor (for_each_group follows ->parent), calling psi_group_change() at each level, which is why a parent's pressure includes its children's and why cgroup.pressure exists to switch the per-level cost off. test_states() turns the four task counts into some/full bits; note NR_MEMSTALL_RUNNING, which keeps a task doing direct reclaim on the CPU from counting as productive. psi_show() prints a zeroed system-wide CPU full line and an IRQ file with only a full line, and psi_account_irqtime() is where IRQ pressure is charged.
PSI triggers and notificationkernel/sched/psi.c (psi_trigger_create, window_update, psi_rtpoll_worker)A write to *.pressure of the form some 150000 1000000 creates a trigger. psi_trigger_create() checks CAP_SYS_RESOURCE on the opener's credentials: privileged triggers are served by a SCHED_FIFO psimon kthread sampling at one-tenth of the smallest window, while unprivileged ones must use whole multiples of two seconds and ride the ordinary averaging work. The last_event_time/pending_event pair in the trigger-update loop is the once-per-window rate limit that delays rather than drops events — the mechanism systemd-oomd and similar userspace daemons build on.
What a container runtime does at the syscall levelkernel/fork.c (copy_process, CLONE_NEW*, CLONE_PIDFD), kernel/cgroup/cgroup.c (cgroup_css_set_fork), fs/namespace.c (path_pivot_root, mount_too_revealing, lock_mnt_tree)There is no single "create a container" syscall; a runtime like runc composes the primitives of this chapter—clone3() with several CLONE_NEW* flags (plus CLONE_INTO_CGROUP so the child is born in its cgroup), writes to uid_map/gid_map, and a pivot_root—in a specific, security-sensitive order. path_pivot_root() shows why the inherited mount tree must first be made private (it returns EINVAL on shared propagation), and mnt_already_visible() is the “fully visible” test that decides whether a user-namespace root may mount a fresh proc or sysfs.
Namespaces do not replace the LSMsecurity/commoncap.c, security/selinux/hooks.c, kernel/seccomp.c, kernel/user_namespace.c (create_user_ns)The chapter's security theme in one fact: namespaces and cgroups only isolate view and usage. Mandatory access control (SELinux, AppArmor, stacked with Landlock) and seccomp are what actually constrain what a process is permitted to do with the single shared kernel every container still runs on top of. In seccomp.c, find the task_no_new_privs() check that makes no_new_privs a precondition for unprivileged filters, and SECCOMP_RET_USER_NOTIF, which hands a system call to a supervisor. create_user_ns() calls security_create_user_ns(), the userns_create hook through which SELinux’s user_namespace { create } permission and AppArmor gate user-namespace creation in mainline.

Chapter 26 — Rust in the Kernel

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
Panics, run-time checks, and build_assert!rust/kernel/lib.rs (#[panic_handler]), rust/kernel/build_assert.rs (build_assert!, build_error!), lib/Kconfig.debug (RUST_DEBUG_ASSERTIONS, RUST_OVERFLOW_CHECKS, RUST_BUILD_ASSERT_ALLOW), Makefile (KBUILD_RUSTFLAGS)What happens when a check fails. KBUILD_RUSTFLAGS compiles every crate with -Cpanic=abort and no unwind tables, so a panic never unwinds into C; the crate’s #[panic_handler] prints the message with pr_emerg! and calls BUG(), so a failed bounds or overflow check becomes an ordinary oops at the faulting line. RUST_OVERFLOW_CHECKS (default y) is what makes integer overflow panic at all. build_assert.rs is the compile-time alternative: build_assert! calls the build_error function, exported as rust_build_error, only on the path where the condition is false, so an assertion the optimizer cannot prove leaves that call in the object file and fails the link unless RUST_BUILD_ASSERT_ALLOW is set.
Toolchain and Kconfig gatingscripts/rust_is_available.sh, scripts/min-tool-version.sh, init/Kconfig (RUST_IS_AVAILABLE, RUST, RUSTC_HAS_*), arch/Kconfig (HAVE_RUST), Documentation/rust/arch-support.rstThe three gates a Rust kernel must pass. rust_is_available.sh (run by make rustavailable) checks that rustc, bindgen, and libclang exist and meet the floors in min-tool-version.sh (rustc 1.78.0, bindgen 0.65.1 in 7.0); only then is RUST_IS_AVAILABLE set. HAVE_RUST is selected per architecture (x86-64, arm64, ARMv7, RISC-V, LoongArch, UML). The RUST entry itself is worth reading for its depends on list — !RANDSTRUCT, !MODVERSIONS || GENDWARFKSYMS, and the RUSTC_VERSION-gated CFI and mitigation constraints — which records exactly which C-side features Rust cannot yet coexist with.
Build pipeline and crate orderrust/Makefile, Makefile (RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP, rustavailable, rust-analyzer), scripts/generate_rust_analyzer.pyrust/Makefile is the whole build in one file: it compiles core from the toolchain’s source with kernel flags, then compiler_builtins, pin_init, ffi, build_error, the generated bindings and uapi crates, and finally kernel, each rule naming its prerequisites so the dependency order is explicit. The host-side proc-macro crates (macros, pin_init_internal) are built against the vendored proc-macro2/quote/syn. The top-level Makefile exporting RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1 is how a stable compiler is allowed to accept the unstable features the crate still uses.
Generated bindings and C helpersrust/bindings/bindings_helper.h, rust/bindings/lib.rs, rust/bindgen_parameters, rust/helpers/helpers.c, rust/helpers/, rust/uapi/bindings_helper.h is the single umbrella header bindgen reads; adding an #include there is how a new C subsystem first becomes visible to Rust. bindgen_parameters lists the types bindgen must treat as opaque or skip. Each file under rust/helpers/ wraps static inline functions and macros (spin_lock, refcount_inc, copy_from_user, …) in real, exported functions, because bindgen sees only what survives preprocessing as a linkable symbol; helpers.c just #includes them all.
Targets and the driver compile rulescripts/generate_rust_target.rs, arch/x86/Makefile, arch/arm64/Makefile (KBUILD_RUSTFLAGS), scripts/Makefile.build (rust_allowed_features, %.o: %.rs), rust/kernel/*.rs.SWhere Rust is made to generate the same code as C. On x86-64 a host program writes scripts/target.json from the configuration (soft-float, retpoline and SLS features), and the arch Makefile disables SSE/AVX and the red zone; arm64 uses rustc’s built-in aarch64-unknown-none-softfloat target. In Makefile.build, the %.o: %.rs rule compiles each driver crate root with no_std, --extern kernel, --sysroot=/dev/null, and the nine-feature unstable allowlist. The .rs.S templates pass the C jump-label and WARN assembly macros through the preprocessor so Rust emits identical __jump_table and __bug_table entries.
Linking glue: exports and FFI typesrust/exports.c (EXPORT_SYMBOL_RUST_GPL), rust/ffi.rs, rust/compiler_builtins.rs, rust/build_error.rsexports.c is a deliberate hack: it includes generated headers listing every Rust symbol and runs each through EXPORT_SYMBOL_GPL, so modules can link against the kernel crate and modpost tracks it like C. Every Rust export is GPL-only by construction, and the v0 symbol-mangling scheme is required because legacy Rust mangling produces names that are not valid C identifiers. ffi.rs fixes the meaning of c_int, c_long, and friends to match the kernel’s C ABI; build_error.rs is the link-time trap behind build_assert!.
The kernel crate and its preluderust/kernel/lib.rs (Module, InPlaceModule, ThisModule), rust/kernel/prelude.rslib.rs is the map of the crate: its 77 pub mod lines, many behind #[cfg(CONFIG_...)], are the coverage list of which C subsystems have safe wrappers in a given configuration. It also defines the Module and InPlaceModule traits and the blanket implementation that turns every Module into an in-place one. prelude.rs shows the vocabulary every driver gets from use kernel::prelude::*.
The safe/unsafe boundaryrust/kernel/types.rs (Opaque, ForeignOwnable), Documentation/rust/coding-guidelines.rst, rust/kernel/safety.rs (unsafe_precondition_assert!)Opaque<T> is the type that lets Rust embed a C struct it must never assume anything about — no validity, no immutability through &, no fixed layout guarantees beyond size — which is how every wrapped C object (locks, devices, misc registrations) is held. ForeignOwnable is how a Rust owner (KBox, Arc) is turned into a void * for a C private_data field and safely recovered later. The coding guidelines define the // SAFETY: comment every unsafe block carries, the /// # Safety contract of every unsafe function and trait, and the # Invariants/// INVARIANT: pair; clippy’s undocumented_unsafe_blocks lint enforces the first. safety.rs, new in 7.0, lets an unsafe fn check its own precondition when RUST_DEBUG_ASSERTIONS is on.
Error handlingrust/kernel/error.rs (Error, code, to_result, from_err_ptr, from_result, to_errno)Error wraps a NonZeroI32 negative errno, so Result<T> costs no more than C’s int return. to_result() converts a C return code at the boundary, from_err_ptr() decodes ERR_PTR values, and from_result() runs in the other direction inside the extern "C" shims the crate generates, turning a Rust Err back into the negative integer the C caller expects. The From impls in the file (AllocError and LayoutError to ENOMEM, TryFromIntError and Utf8Error to EINVAL) are what let ? be applied to allocation and conversion calls, and the declare_err! list shows which of the 55 errno codes Rust has needed so far.
Fallible, GFP-aware allocationrust/kernel/alloc.rs (Flags, Allocator, NumaNode), rust/kernel/alloc/allocator.rs (Kmalloc, Vmalloc, KVmalloc), rust/kernel/alloc/kbox.rs, rust/kernel/alloc/kvec.rsThe kernel replaces Rust’s infallible global allocator with an Allocator trait whose every call takes GFP Flags and returns a Result. allocator.rs binds the three backends to krealloc_node_align, vrealloc_node_align, and kvrealloc_node_align, and kbox.rs/kvec.rs are generic over them, which is where the KBox/VBox/KVBox and KVec/VVec/KVVec aliases come from. This layer replaced the standard alloc crate in 6.13, so there is no global allocator. Kmalloc::aligned_layout shows how over-aligned types are fitted to kmalloc’s alignment rules, and KVec::reserve with push_within_capacity is the allocate-before-locking idiom.
Reference countingrust/kernel/sync/arc.rs (Arc, ArcBorrow, UniqueArc), rust/kernel/sync/aref.rs (ARef, AlwaysRefCounted), rust/kernel/sync/refcount.rsTwo models side by side. Arc is Rust-owned shared memory whose count is a kernel refcount_t (so saturation, not wraparound, on overflow), with ArcBorrow as the cheap borrowed handle and UniqueArc for the not-yet-shared phase of construction. ARef is for C objects that already carry their own count: implementing AlwaysRefCounted for a wrapper type maps inc_ref/dec_ref onto the C get/put pair; grep "AlwaysRefCounted for" lists the 22 C types wrapped this way in 7.0 (plus one test type). The Opaque definition in rust/kernel/types.rs and the Device wrapper in rust/kernel/device.rs show how a C object is embedded and reborrowed with from_raw.
Pinning and in-place initializationrust/pin-init/src/lib.rs (PinInit, Init, pin_init_from_closure), rust/pin-init/internal/src/lib.rs (pin_data, pin_init, pinned_drop), rust/kernel/init.rs (InPlaceInit, try_pin_init)pin-init is a standalone library vendored into the tree: PinInit is an initializer that writes a value directly into its final, pinned location, which is what makes self-referential C primitives (a struct mutex with its wait list, a list_head) safe to embed. The #[pin_data] and pin_init! proc macros generate the per-field projection and the drop-guard that unwinds partially initialized fields on error. rust/kernel/init.rs adapts it to the kernel’s Error type and adds InPlaceInit so KBox and Arc can be pin-initialized with GFP flags. The library was merged in 6.4, moved to rust/pin-init/ in 6.15, and had its macros rewritten on syn in 7.0, so rust/pin-init/internal/src/init.rs is where the <- field syntax and the drop guards are now generated.
Locks that own their datarust/kernel/sync/lock.rs (Lock, Backend, Guard), rust/kernel/sync/lock/mutex.rs, rust/kernel/sync/lock/spinlock.rs, rust/kernel/sync/locked_by.rs, rust/kernel/sync/lock/global.rs, rust/kernel/sync/condvar.rs, rust/kernel/sync.rs (LockClassKey, static_lock_class)Lock<T, B> is one generic type: the data T lives inside the lock, and the Backend trait (implemented by mutex.rs and spinlock.rs over the C primitives) supplies lock and unlock. The only path to T is through a Guard, whose Drop unlocks. LockedBy covers data protected by a lock that lives elsewhere, and LockClassKey, produced per call site by static_lock_class! inside new_mutex!/new_spinlock!, is how every Rust lock gets its own lockdep class. LockedBy::access shows the one run-time check (the owner’s address), and global.rs provides global_lock! for static locks.
Other synchronization primitivesrust/kernel/sync/atomic.rs, rust/kernel/sync/atomic/ordering.rs, rust/kernel/sync/barrier.rs, rust/kernel/sync/completion.rs, rust/kernel/sync/rcu.rs, rust/kernel/sync/set_once.rsThe rest of Chapter 5 in Rust. Atomic<T> (6.18) implements LKMM atomics by calling the generated C atomic helpers, and takes the memory ordering as a zero-sized type argument, so a wrong ordering for an operation (for example a release load) is a type error. barrier.rs maps smp_mb/smp_rmb/smp_wmb; completion.rs wraps struct completion; rcu.rs turns rcu_read_lock/rcu_read_unlock into a guard; set_once.rs is a write-once cell built on an atomic.
The procedural macrosrust/macros/lib.rs, rust/macros/module.rs, rust/macros/vtable.rs, rust/syn/, rust/quote/, rust/proc-macro2/, rust/kernel/module_param.rs, rust/kernel/driver.rs (module_driver!), rust/exports.cmodule.rs expands module! into the .modinfo entries, the init_module/cleanup_module (or built-in initcall) entry points, and parameter storage, calling the type through InPlaceModule::init. vtable.rs adds a HAS_<METHOD> constant for every trait method an impl actually defines, so the crate can leave a C function pointer NULL rather than install a stub — preserving the “absent op means default behavior” convention C subsystems rely on. Both macros were converted to syn in 7.0; parse_ordered_fields! in module.rs is where the key order and the error messages come from. module_param.rs (6.19) shows integer parameters stored in a SetOnce with permission 0. module_driver! is the Rust counterpart of C’s module_driver(), wrapping a bus driver::Registration in an InPlaceModule. exports.c shows every Rust symbol exported with EXPORT_SYMBOL_GPL.
Misc devices and user-space accessrust/kernel/miscdevice.rs (MiscDevice, MiscDeviceRegistration, MiscdeviceVTable), rust/kernel/uaccess.rs (UserPtr, UserSlice, UserSliceReader, UserSliceWriter), rust/kernel/fs/kiocb.rs (Kiocb), rust/kernel/iov.rs (IovIterSource, IovIterDest), rust/kernel/ioctl.rs, samples/rust/rust_misc_device.rs, samples/rust/rust_minimal.rs, samples/rust/KconfigThe clearest small example of the whole pattern. MiscdeviceVTable builds a C file_operations whose extern "C" functions recover the Rust object from private_data and call the trait methods, filling only the slots the #[vtable] impl provides; its VTABLE constant is evaluated at compile time, and compat_ioctl falls back to compat_ptr_ioctl as in C drivers. MiscDeviceRegistration wraps misc_register/misc_deregister in a pinned value. UserSlice splits a user buffer into a reader and a writer that track how much remains, so an ioctl cannot over-read or over-write the region user space passed; a transfer that does not fit fails with EFAULT. Kiocb and the two iov_iter wrappers carry read_iter/write_iter, with simple_read_from_buffer and copy_from_iter_vec as the common helpers, and ioctl.rs re-creates the _IOC encoding as const fns. Each sample has its own tristate SAMPLE_RUST_* option under the SAMPLES_RUST menu.
Bus drivers, device contexts, and Devressamples/rust/rust_driver_pci.rs, rust/kernel/pci.rs (Driver, Adapter, pci_device_table!, module_pci_driver!), rust/kernel/pci/io.rs (Bar, iomap_region_sized), rust/kernel/device.rs (Normal, Bound, Core, CoreInternal), rust/kernel/devres.rs, rust/kernel/revocable.rs, rust/kernel/io.rs, rust/kernel/driver.rs, drivers/base/dd.c (device_unbind_cleanup)The template every Rust bus driver follows. The adapter’s probe_callback runs the driver’s PinInit into a pinned KBox and stores it as driver data; remove_callback calls unbind, and the post_unbind_rust hook drops the data after devres_release_all(). The impl_device_context_deref! chain lets Core stand in for Bound and Bound for Normal. Devres::new registers a devm_add_action callback that revokes the inner Revocable (with synchronize_rcu) on unbind; Devres::access skips RCU for a matching &Device<Bound>. offset_valid plus build_assert! checks constant MMIO offsets at compile time, and the try_* accessors return EINVAL at run time.
Real Rust driversdrivers/android/binder/, drivers/gpu/drm/nova/, drivers/gpu/nova-core/, drivers/gpu/drm/tyr/, drivers/gpu/drm/drm_panic_qr.rs, drivers/net/phy/ax88796b_rust.rs, drivers/net/phy/qt2025.rs, drivers/block/rnull/, drivers/cpufreq/rcpufreq_dt.rs, drivers/pwm/pwm_th1520.rs, drivers/android/binder/rust_binderfs.c, drivers/android/binder/rust_binder_events.c, drivers/block/rnull/configfs.rs, mm/kasan/kasan_test_rust.rs, lib/find_bit_benchmark_rust.rs, Documentation/rust/general-information.rst, Documentation/rust/index.rst, MAINTAINERSRead in increasing size: the PHY drivers show a complete Rust driver in little more than a hundred lines; rnull shows the block abstractions (and a configfs interface); nova-core and Tyr show greenfield GPU drivers whose structure was chosen freely; and Binder, selected by ANDROID_BINDER_IPC_RUST as a build-time alternative to the C driver, shows a whole security-critical subsystem written mostly in safe Rust; its C files (rust_binderfs.c, rust_binder_events.c) show where Rust still leans on C, and deferred_close.rs shows driver-local code calling raw bindings where no abstraction exists yet. drm_panic_qr.rs (through #[export] functions) and kasan_test_rust.rs (through a #[no_mangle] function) show Rust called from inside C subsystems. general-information.rst states the rule that leaf drivers should not use C bindings directly; git log Documentation/rust/index.rst records the end of the experiment, and grep -n "\.rs" MAINTAINERS shows who maintains each Rust file. Comparing rcpufreq_dt.rs with the C cpufreq-dt.c beside it is the most direct side-by-side of the two languages for the same job.

Chapter 27 — Kernel Security Infrastructure

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
LSM blobs and hook dispatchsecurity/lsm_init.c, security/security.c, include/linux/lsm_hooks.hlsm_prepare() and lsm_blob_size_update() show how each module's struct lsm_blob_sizes becomes an offset into one shared allocation, and lsm_cred_alloc() and lsm_inode_alloc() show the allocation itself. Then read security_file_open() and call_int_hook() to see how one hook call runs every module's callback. lsm_order_parse() turns lsm= and CONFIG_LSM into the module order, and security_inode_init_security() shows each module filling its reserved xattr slots for a new file.
SELinux access decisionssecurity/selinux/hooks.c, security/selinux/avc.c, security/selinux/ss/services.cFollow selinux_inode_permission() into avc_has_perm_noaudit(). A miss in the access vector cache falls through to security_compute_av() in the policy server. inode_doinit_with_dentry() shows how a file's label is read from its security.selinux extended attribute.
AppArmor and Smacksecurity/apparmor/lsm.c, security/apparmor/file.c, security/apparmor/match.c, security/smack/smack_lsm.capparmor_file_open() calls aa_path_perm(), which matches a pathname against a profile's DFA with aa_dfa_match(). That is the path-based model, in contrast to SELinux's labels. In Smack, smk_access() shows how simple a label-comparison model can be.
TOMOYO and the single-purpose LSMssecurity/tomoyo/domain.c, security/yama/yama_lsm.c, security/loadpin/loadpin.c, security/safesetid/lsm.c, security/bpf/hooks.c, kernel/bpf/bpf_lsm.ctomoyo_find_next_domain() builds a domain name from the exec history. yama_ptrace_access_check(), loadpin_check() and safesetid_task_fix_setuid() are each a whole module's policy in one function. security/bpf/hooks.c registers a stub for every hook, and bpf_lsm_verify_prog() decides which of them a BPF program may attach to.
Crypto API core and lookupcrypto/api.c, crypto/algapi.c, include/linux/crypto.hcrypto_alg_mod_lookup(), crypto_larval_add() and crypto_larval_wait() show how a missing algorithm is requested, built from a template and waited for. crypto_register_alg() and crypto_register_template() show how a provider enters crypto_alg_list with its cra_priority. crypto_start_tests() launches the boot-time tests that were deferred until crypto_algapi_init(), and crypto_unregister_alg(), crypto_remove_alg() and crypto_remove_spawns() show the reverse path that marks an algorithm dead and tears down the instances built on it. crypto_create_tfm_node() is where a transform is allocated and initialized.
Hash and cipher transformsinclude/crypto/hash.h, crypto/shash.c, crypto/ahash.c, crypto/skcipher.c, crypto/aead.cStart with crypto_alloc_shash() and crypto_shash_digest(), the synchronous interface most kernel users need. Then compare the request-based ahash_request and skcipher_request flow and crypto_wait_req(), which turns an asynchronous completion into a blocking call. In crypto/ahash.c, ahash_do_req_chain() shows the 6.16 fallback that exports the hash state, finishes on a synchronous transform and imports it back, and crypto_clone_ahash() is the cloning call TCP-AO relies on.
Asynchronous requests and crypto enginesinclude/crypto/internal/skcipher.h, crypto/skcipher.c, crypto/crypto_engine.c, include/crypto/engine.h, security/keys/trusted-keys/trusted_dcp.cskcipher_walk_virt() and skcipher_walk_done() show how a CPU implementation maps a scatterlist one chunk at a time. crypto_transfer_skcipher_request_to_engine() queues a request for a hardware driver, and crypto_pump_requests() hands queued requests one at a time to the driver's do_one_request() from a kthread worker; crypto_finalize_request() completes them. In trusted_dcp.c, do_dcp_crypto() (which sets CRYPTO_TFM_REQ_MAY_BACKLOG) and do_aead_crypto() wrap each call in crypto_wait_req(), the idiom that turns -EINPROGRESS and -EBUSY back into a blocking call.
Templates, cryptomgr and self-testscrypto/algboss.c, crypto/testmgr.c, crypto/hmac.c, crypto/fips.ccryptomgr_schedule_probe() and cryptomgr_probe() instantiate a template such as hmac on demand. alg_test() runs the known-answer vectors before an algorithm is marked tested, and fips_enabled restricts the choice to approved algorithms; alg_fips_disabled() returns the -ECANCELED that makes the core mark an algorithm CRYPTO_ALG_FIPS_INTERNAL.
Architecture-optimized cryptoarch/x86/crypto/aesni-intel_glue.c, arch/arm64/crypto/aes-glue.c, lib/crypto/sha256.c, lib/crypto/x86/sha256.h, lib/crypto/arm64/sha256.hThe glue files register AES-NI and ARMv8 Crypto Extensions drivers at high cra_priority and bracket the SIMD code with kernel_fpu_begin() on x86 or scoped_ksimd() on arm64. In lib/crypto, the library functions choose the architecture code once at boot with static keys, without any lookup, and lib/crypto/fips.h holds the vectors for the power-on self-tests the SHA libraries run when fips_enabled is set. crypto/mldsa.c is a minimal example of an API entry that only wraps the verifier in lib/crypto/mldsa.c.
Kernel random number generatordrivers/char/random.c, include/linux/random.h, crypto/drbg.cStart with crng_make_state() and crng_fast_key_erasure(), the path every get_random_bytes() and get_random_u32() call takes through a per-CPU ChaCha20 key. crng_reseed() refills base_crng from extract_entropy(), which expands the BLAKE2s input pool. On the input side, add_interrupt_randomness() and mix_interrupt_randomness() feed the pool, _credit_init_bits() moves crng_init to CRNG_READY, and try_to_generate_entropy() is the jitter fallback. SYSCALL_DEFINE3(getrandom, ...), random_read_iter() and urandom_read_iter() show the user-space semantics, and drbg_seed_from_random() shows the crypto API’s DRBG seeding itself from this generator.
Hardware entropy sourcesarch/x86/include/asm/archrandom.h, arch/x86/kernel/cpu/rdrand.c, arch/arm64/include/asm/archrandom.h, drivers/char/hw_random/core.c, drivers/virt/vmgenid.crandom_init_early() in drivers/char/random.c calls arch_get_random_seed_longs() and arch_get_random_longs(), which are rdseed_long() and rdrand_long() on x86_64; x86_init_rdrand() disables both if the instruction returns stuck values. On ARM64 the seed call tries the SMCCC TRNG firmware service (smccc_trng_available) before RNDRRS. hwrng_fillfn() is the kernel thread that feeds TPM, virtio-rng and SoC generators to add_hwgenerator_randomness(), and vmgenid_notify() calls add_vmfork_randomness() when a VM is cloned. cc_random_init() in arch/x86/coco/core.c seeds the pool from RDRAND in confidential-computing guests.
User-space crypto interfacecrypto/af_alg.c, crypto/algif_hash.c, crypto/algif_skcipher.c, crypto/proc.calg_bind() resolves the name a sockaddr_alg carries, and af_alg_accept() creates the operation socket. crypto/proc.c prints /proc/crypto, one entry per registered implementation.
Keys, keyrings and the key syscallsinclude/linux/key.h, security/keys/key.c, security/keys/keyctl.c, security/keys/keyring.c, security/keys/process_keys.c, security/keys/persistent.cstruct key and struct key_type define the model. SYSCALL_DEFINE5(add_key, ...) leads to key_create_or_update(). search_cred_keyrings_rcu() searches the thread, process and session keyrings in order, and key_task_permission() applies possessor, user, group and other permissions. keyring_search_rcu() walks nested keyrings up to KEYRING_SEARCH_MAX_DEPTH; keyctl_get_persistent(), keyctl_keyring_move(), keyctl_capabilities() and keyctl_watch_key() implement the newer operations.
request_key and key garbage collectionsecurity/keys/request_key.c, security/keys/request_key_auth.c, security/keys/gc.crequest_key_tag() calls construct_key_and_link(). That function builds an authorization key and runs /sbin/request-key through call_usermodehelper_exec(). complete_request_key() wakes the waiters. request_key_rcu() searches without sleeping, and check_cached_key()/cache_requested_key() implement CONFIG_KEYS_REQUEST_CACHE. key_garbage_collector() reaps expired, revoked and dead-type keys from a work item.
System trusted keyrings and platform certificatescerts/system_keyring.c, certs/blacklist.c, security/integrity/platform_certs/load_uefi.c, security/integrity/platform_certs/keyring_handler.cverify_pkcs7_signature() is the single entry point that module, kexec and dm-verity signature checks share. Its trusted_keys argument selects the built-in, secondary or platform keyring. load_uefi_certs() imports UEFI db, dbx and the MOK lists, and get_handler_for_mok() decides between .machine and .platform using imputed_trust_enabled(). restrict_link_by_builtin_secondary_and_machine() and restrict_link_by_digsig_builtin() guard the secondary and IMA keyrings, and is_hash_blacklisted() checks tbs: and bin: entries.
X.509, PKCS#7 and public-key signaturescrypto/asymmetric_keys/x509_cert_parser.c, crypto/asymmetric_keys/x509_public_key.c, crypto/asymmetric_keys/pkcs7_verify.c, crypto/asymmetric_keys/pkcs7_trust.c, crypto/asymmetric_keys/public_key.c, crypto/asymmetric_keys/restrict.cx509_cert_parse() decodes a certificate with the ASN.1 decoder and calls x509_check_for_self_signed(), and x509_key_preparse() turns the result into an asymmetric key. pkcs7_verify() hashes the signed content and checks each signer; pkcs7_validate_trust() walks the chain until a certificate matches a key on the chosen keyring. Both end in public_key_verify_signature(), which allocates a crypto_sig transform for RSA, ECDSA or ML-DSA. restrict_link_by_signature() is the rule that lets only properly signed certificates join the secondary keyring.
Signed BPF programskernel/bpf/syscall.c, kernel/bpf/helpers.c, include/linux/bpf.hbpf_prog_verify_signature() (6.18) runs from bpf_prog_load() before security_bpf_prog_load() and the verifier when union bpf_attr carries a signature. It resolves keyring_id with system_keyring_id_check() and bpf_lookup_system_key() or bpf_lookup_user_key(), then calls bpf_verify_pkcs7_signature() over the instruction array, which reaches verify_pkcs7_signature(). The sha field of struct bpf_map and excl_prog_sha are what a signed loader uses to check the maps it is handed.
Trusted keys and the TPMsecurity/keys/trusted-keys/trusted_core.c, security/keys/trusted-keys/trusted_tpm2.c, security/keys/trusted-keys/trusted_tee.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm-interface.ctrusted_instantiate() dispatches to whichever trusted_key_ops backend was chosen at boot. tpm2_seal_trusted() wraps a key under the TPM's storage hierarchy, and tpm_pcr_extend() is the call IMA uses to extend a PCR.
Encrypted keyssecurity/keys/encrypted-keys/encrypted.c, security/keys/encrypted-keys/masterkey_trusted.cencrypted_instantiate() parses the new or load command and calls request_master_key() to find the trusted or user key that protects the payload. get_derived_key() derives separate encryption and authentication keys from it with SHA-256, derived_key_encrypt() encrypts the payload with cbc(aes) through the crypto API, and datablob_hmac_append() adds the HMAC-SHA256 that is checked again when the blob is loaded.
TPM driver coredrivers/char/tpm/tpm-chip.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm-interface.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm2-cmd.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm2-sessions.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm_tis_core.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm_crb.ctpm_chip_register() publishes a chip as /dev/tpm0 (plus /dev/tpmrm0 for TPM 2.0) and registers it as a hardware RNG through tpm_add_hwrng(). tpm_transmit_cmd() is the single path every in-kernel command takes, and tpm_pcr_extend(), tpm2_pcr_extend() and tpm_get_random() are the calls IMA, trusted keys and the RNG use. tpm2_start_auth_session() sets up the encrypted HMAC session that protects bus traffic, and tpm_tis_send() and crb_send() show the two common hardware interfaces.
Confidential-guest attestation and virtual TPMsdrivers/virt/coco/guest/report.c, drivers/virt/coco/sev-guest/sev-guest.c, drivers/virt/coco/tdx-guest/tdx-guest.c, drivers/virt/coco/arm-cca-guest/arm-cca-guest.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm_svsm.c, drivers/char/tpm/tpm_crb_ffa.ctsm_report_register() (6.7) lets one back end supply the report_new() operation behind the configfs TSM_REPORTS directory; tsm_report_read() generates the report when user space reads outblob. sev_report_new(), tdx_report_new() and arm_cca_report_new() are the three architecture back ends. tpm_svsm_send() (6.16) passes TPM commands to an SEV-SNP guest's SVSM through snp_svsm_vtpm_send_command(), and tpm_crb_ffa_init() (6.15) carries the CRB interface over Arm FF-A.
PCI device security (TSM and TDISP)drivers/pci/tsm.c, drivers/virt/coco/tsm-core.c, drivers/pci/ide.c, drivers/crypto/ccp/sev-dev-tsm.cAll new in 6.19. tsm_register() creates a /sys/class/tsm/tsmN device and calls pci_tsm_register(). A write to a device's tsm/connect attribute reaches connect_store() and pci_tsm_connect(), which call the back end's connect operation; in sev-dev-tsm.c, dsm_connect() relays SPDM messages between the AMD secure processor and the device's PCIe DOE mailbox in sev_tio_spdm_cmd(), then turns on IDE link encryption through streams_enable(), which calls pci_ide_stream_enable() for the root port and the device. pci_tsm_bind() is the interface for assigning a device interface to a confidential VM; it has no in-tree caller in 7.0.
IMA measurement and appraisalsecurity/integrity/ima/ima_main.c, security/integrity/ima/ima_policy.c, security/integrity/ima/ima_queue.c, security/integrity/ima/ima_appraise.cprocess_measurement() is the heart of IMA. It calls ima_get_action() to match policy rules, ima_collect_measurement() to hash the file, and then ima_store_measurement(), ima_appraise_measurement() or ima_audit_measurement(). ima_add_template_entry() appends to the measurement list and extends the PCR. Then read ima_check_last_writer() and ima_rdwr_violation_check() for cache invalidation and violations, ima_update_xattr() for how a hash in security.ima is kept current, default_measurement_rules and secure_boot_rules in ima_policy.c for the built-in policies, ima_template.c for the template list, and ima_kexec.c for how the list crosses a kexec.
EVMsecurity/integrity/evm/evm_main.c, security/integrity/evm/evm_crypto.cevm_verifyxattr() checks security.evm against an HMAC or signature computed over the protected extended attributes, which evm_calc_hmac() produces. The evm_inode_setxattr() hook keeps an attacker from changing security.ima without also updating the EVM value. evm_config_default_xattrnames[] lists the protected xattrs, evm_inode_init_security() labels new files, evm_inode_copy_up_xattr() decides what survives an overlayfs copy-up, and evm_write_key() in evm_secfs.c enforces the initialization-flag rules.
fs-verity, dm-verity and IPEfs/verity/enable.c, fs/verity/verify.c, drivers/md/dm-verity-target.c, security/ipe/hooks.c, security/ipe/eval.cfsverity_ioctl_enable() builds the Merkle tree and verify_data_block() walks it upward on reads. verity_verify_io() does the same for a block device. ipe_evaluate_event() applies an IPE policy to the properties those two record for each file and each device. Then read ensure_verity_info() and fsverity_set_info() in fs/verity/open.c for how the verity information is set up at open time and inserted in the rhashtable (7.0), fsverity_readahead() (7.0) for hash readahead, and fsverity_verify_signature() in fs/verity/signature.c for the built-in signature check. On the dm-verity side, verity_verify_root_hash() in drivers/md/dm-verity-verify-sig.c chooses the keyrings, including the .dm-verity keyring (7.0), and verity_recheck() and verity_handle_err() handle a mismatch. For IPE, ipe_bprm_creds_for_exec() (6.19) handles AT_EXECVE_CHECK, and ipe_update_policy() and ipe_set_active_pol() in security/ipe/policy.c enforce the name and version rules.
Audit pipelinekernel/audit.c, kernel/auditsc.c, kernel/auditfilter.c, security/lsm_audit.caudit_log_start(), audit_log_format() and audit_log_end() build and queue a record, and kauditd_thread() sends it to auditd over NETLINK_AUDIT. __audit_syscall_exit() and audit_filter_syscall() produce the per-system-call records, and common_lsm_audit() formats AVC records. Then read audit_receive_msg() for the control commands and the namespace and capability checks, auditd_set() and audit_replace() for daemon registration, kauditd_hold_skb() and kauditd_retry_skb() for the fallback queues, audit_enable() for the audit= boot parameter, audit_set_loginuid() for the login UID and session ID, and audit_signal_info() for recording who signals auditd. tty_audit_add_data() in drivers/tty/tty_audit.c collects TTY input.
Audit watches and treeskernel/audit_watch.c, kernel/audit_tree.c, kernel/audit_fsnotify.c, kernel/auditsc.caudit_add_watch() attaches a file rule to an fsnotify mark on the parent directory, and audit_watch_handle_event() updates the rule when the file is renamed or replaced. audit_add_tree_rule() and audit_tag_tree() mark every mount under a directory for -F dir= rules, and audit_tree_handle_event() follows changes. audit_alloc_mark() backs exe rules, and audit_filter_inodes() matches the inodes a system call touched against all of them at exit. audit_match_perm() in kernel/auditsc.c and audit_classify_syscall() in lib/audit.c (ARM64) or arch/x86/kernel/audit_64.c implement the -p permission classes, and audit_trim_trees() and audit_tag_tree() serve AUDIT_TRIM and AUDIT_MAKE_EQUIV.
Kernel lockdownsecurity/lockdown/lockdown.c, include/linux/security.h, drivers/char/mem.clockdown_is_locked_down() compares a lockdown_reason against the current level. The enumeration in include/linux/security.h is ordered so that every integrity reason comes before LOCKDOWN_INTEGRITY_MAX. open_port() is a typical caller of security_locked_down(). Then read the callers that pick a safe variant: debugfs_locked_down() in fs/debugfs/file.c, param_check_unsafe() in kernel/params.c, kimage_validate_signature() in kernel/kexec_file.c, bpf_base_func_proto() in kernel/bpf/helpers.c for the load-time BPF checks, and restrict_driver() in drivers/xen/privcmd.c for the 7.0 Xen reason.
Module loading through the layerskernel/module/main.c, kernel/module/signing.c, fs/kernel_read_file.c, arch/x86/kernel/module.cfinit_module() calls may_init_module() (CAP_SYS_MODULE, modules_disabled) and then init_module_from_file(), whose kernel_read_file() runs security_kernel_read_file() and security_kernel_post_read_file() with READING_MODULE. load_module() calls module_sig_check(), which uses mod_verify_sig() and falls back to security_locked_down(LOCKDOWN_MODULE_SIGNATURE) for unsigned modules, then audit_log_kern_module(). module_enable_rodata_ro(), module_enable_data_nx() and module_enable_text_rox() apply W^X, and x86 module_finalize() calls apply_fineibt() and apply_seal_endbr().
Secure Boot state and arch policydrivers/firmware/efi/libstub/secureboot.c, arch/x86/kernel/setup.c, security/integrity/ima/ima_efi.c, security/lockdown/lockdown.cefi_get_secureboot() in the EFI stub combines the firmware’s SecureBoot and SetupMode state (through efi_get_secureboot_mode()) with shim’s MokSBStateRT, and on x86_64 the result travels in boot_params.secure_boot, which setup_arch() reports. arch_ima_get_secureboot() and arch_get_ima_policy() turn that state into mandatory module and kexec appraisal rules. lockdown_param() and lock_kernel_down() show that mainline raises the lockdown level only from the command line, Kconfig or securityfs. load_uefi_certs() in security/integrity/platform_certs/load_uefi.c shows which variables are read only under Secure Boot, and allocate_new_fdt_and_exit_boot() in drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/fdt.c shows the ARM64 stub ignoring dtb= under Secure Boot.
Hardeningsecurity/Kconfig.hardening, mm/usercopy.c, init/main.c, arch/x86/kernel/alternative.c, arch/arm64/include/asm/pointer_auth.h, include/linux/ucopysize.h, include/linux/fortify-string.h, include/linux/slab.h, include/linux/randomize_kstack.h, arch/x86/kernel/cpu/common.c, kernel/configs/hardening.configThe Kconfig file groups the compiler-based options. __check_object_size() implements hardened usercopy, and mark_readonly() write-protects __ro_after_init data. cfi_parse_cmdline() and apply_seal_endbr() show the x86 kCFI/FineIBT and IBT setup, and struct ptrauth_keys_kernel holds ARM64's kernel PAC key. check_copy_size() is the always-built size check on user copies, fortify_memcpy_chk() the fortified memcpy() with its field-spanning warning, and kmalloc_obj() and __alloc_flex() the 7.0 typed allocators. choose_random_kstack_offset() picks the per-task stack offset, native_write_cr4() pins CR4 bits and setup_lass() enables LASS, and the hardening fragment is what make hardening.config merges.
Stack canaries and slab hardeninginclude/linux/stackprotector.h, arch/x86/include/asm/stackprotector.h, arch/arm64/include/asm/stackprotector.h, kernel/fork.c, mm/slub.cget_random_canary() takes the canary from the RNG, and boot_init_stack_canary() installs it per architecture; kernel/fork.c gives each new task its own stack_canary. In SLUB, freelist_ptr_encode() XORs each free pointer with the per-cache s->random and its own address under CONFIG_SLAB_FREELIST_HARDENED, and init_cache_random_seq() and shuffle_freelist() randomize object order under CONFIG_SLAB_FREELIST_RANDOM.
ARM64 control-flow protectionarch/arm64/include/asm/pointer_auth.h, arch/arm64/kernel/cpufeature.c, arch/arm64/kernel/pi/patch-scs.c, kernel/scs.c, kernel/cfi.c, arch/arm64/kernel/traps.cptrauth_keys_init_kernel() and ptrauth_keys_switch_kernel() load the per-task kernel PAC key. bti_enable() in cpufeature.c turns on Branch Target Identification for kernel text. scs_alloc() gives each task a shadow call stack, and scs_patch() rewrites PAC instructions into shadow-stack pushes at boot on CPUs without PAC. A kCFI mismatch traps into report_cfi_failure(), called from the ARM64 BRK handler in traps.c.
Selecting and reporting CPU vulnerability mitigationskernel/cpu.c, arch/x86/kernel/cpu/bugs.c, arch/arm64/kernel/proton-pack.c, drivers/base/cpu.cmitigations_parse_cmdline() turns mitigations= into the global setting and, since 6.17, the per-attack-vector switches that cpu_attack_vector_mitigated() reports. On x86_64, cpu_select_mitigations() runs a select, update and apply step for each vulnerability; on ARM64, spectre_v4_enable_mitigation() and spectre_bhb_enable_mitigation() run as CPU capabilities are enabled. cpu_root_vulnerabilities_attrs builds /sys/devices/system/cpu/vulnerabilities/, where any file the architecture does not provide falls back to cpu_show_not_affected().

Chapter 28 — Virtualization: KVM

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
The /dev/kvm ioctl layersvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, include/uapi/linux/kvm.hStart at kvm_dev_ioctl(), kvm_vm_ioctl(), and kvm_vcpu_ioctl(), the three dispatchers behind the system, VM, and vCPU file descriptors. The UAPI header defines every ioctl, capability, and the shared struct kvm_run.
Enabling hardware virtualizationvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, arch/x86/kvm/vmx/vmx.c, arch/x86/kvm/svm/svm.c, arch/arm64/kvm/arm.c, arch/x86/kernel/reboot.ckvm_enable_virtualization() reference-counts per-CPU enabling and, with enable_virt_at_load (6.12, the default), runs at module load. kvm_arch_enable_virtualization_cpu() reaches vmx_enable_virtualization_cpu() (VMXON) or svm_enable_virtualization_cpu() (EFER.SVME) on x86 and cpu_hyp_init() on ARM64. cpu_emergency_disable_virtualization() leaves VMX or SVM before a reboot or crash kexec so that INIT works.
Core VM and vCPU objectsinclude/linux/kvm_host.hRead struct kvm, struct kvm_vcpu, and struct kvm_memory_slot, plus the request helpers kvm_make_request() and kvm_check_request().
VM lifetime, statistics, and fatal errorsvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, virt/kvm/binary_stats.c, include/linux/kvm_host.hkvm_dev_ioctl_create_vm() reserves the descriptor, builds the VM with kvm_create_vm(), and installs the file last. kvm_put_kvm() drops users_count and calls kvm_destroy_vm() at zero. kvm_stats_read() serves the header, descriptors, and data of a statistics fd, and KVM_BUG_ON() calls kvm_vm_bugged() to mark a VM dead so that every later ioctl returns -EIO.
Guest CPU modelarch/x86/kvm/cpuid.c, arch/arm64/kvm/sys_regs.ckvm_dev_ioctl_get_cpuid() answers KVM_GET_SUPPORTED_CPUID and KVM_GET_EMULATED_CPUID. kvm_set_cpuid() installs a vCPU's table, accepting only an identical one after the first run, and kvm_vcpu_after_set_cpuid() derives the guest's capabilities from it. On ARM64, set_id_reg() validates a VMM write to an ID register and rejects a changed value with -EBUSY once the VM has run, and kvm_vm_ioctl_get_reg_writable_masks() reports which fields may change.
Requests and kicksvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, include/linux/kvm_host.hkvm_vcpu_kick() wakes a blocked vCPU or sends an IPI to force a running one out of guest mode, and kvm_vcpu_exiting_guest_mode() avoids sending the IPI twice. kvm_make_all_cpus_request() posts a request such as a TLB flush to every vCPU and, for requests marked KVM_REQUEST_WAIT, waits until vCPUs in guest mode have left it.
x86 run looparch/x86/kvm/x86.ckvm_arch_vcpu_ioctl_run() calls vcpu_run(), which loops over vcpu_enter_guest(): request processing, the vendor vcpu_run static call, and exit handling.
x86 exit fastpath and tracingarch/x86/kvm/x86.c, arch/x86/kvm/vmx/vmx.c, arch/x86/kvm/trace.hhandle_fastpath_wrmsr(), handle_fastpath_hlt(), and handle_fastpath_invd() finish common exits with interrupts still disabled, and vmx_exit_handlers_fastpath() chooses among them; the fastpath_t they return drives the inner loop of vcpu_enter_guest(). The kvm_entry and kvm_exit tracepoints record every transition and feed perf kvm stat.
x86 instruction emulatorarch/x86/kvm/emulate.c, arch/x86/kvm/x86.cx86_emulate_instruction() is the entry for exits that need a guest instruction decoded, such as MMIO without a fast path. x86_decode_insn() decodes the instruction from guest memory, and x86_emulate_insn() executes it through callbacks that may end in an MMIO or port I/O exit to the VMM.
x86 guest entry and vendor dispatcharch/x86/include/asm/kvm-x86-ops.h, arch/x86/kvm/vmx/main.c, arch/x86/kvm/vmx/vmenter.S, arch/x86/kvm/svm/vmenter.S, kernel/entry/virt.cThe header lists every KVM_X86_OP hook that becomes a static call. vt_x86_ops in vmx/main.c routes each hook to VMX or to TDX. __vmx_vcpu_run and __svm_vcpu_run are the assembly that loads guest registers and executes VMLAUNCH/VMRESUME or VMRUN. xfer_to_guest_mode_handle_work() handles pending signals and rescheduling before each entry.
Intel VMXarch/x86/kvm/vmx/vmx.c, arch/x86/kvm/vmx/nested.cvmx_vcpu_run() enters the guest and __vmx_handle_exit() dispatches through kvm_vmx_exit_handlers[]. The nested file emulates VMX for an L1 hypervisor.
AMD SVM and SEVarch/x86/kvm/svm/svm.c, arch/x86/kvm/svm/sev.csvm_vcpu_run() and svm_handle_exit() with svm_exit_handlers[] mirror VMX around the VMCB. snp_launch_update() and sev_gmem_prepare() show SEV-SNP on guest_memfd, and sev_handle_vmgexit() dispatches the guest's GHCB requests, including snp_handle_ext_guest_req() for attestation.
Intel TDX hostarch/x86/kvm/vmx/tdx.ctdx_vcpu_run() enters a trust domain through the TDX module instead of VMLAUNCH, and the file shows which exits KVM may still see. tdx_vcpu_init_mem_region() and tdx_td_finalize() build and measure a TD, tdx_sept_remove_private_spte() shows the block, track, and remove sequence, and handle_tdvmcall() lists the TDVMCALL leaves KVM handles.
Memslots and MMU notifiersvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, include/linux/kvm_host.hkvm_set_memory_region() validates a KVM_SET_USER_MEMORY_REGION2 call, and kvm_set_memslot() publishes the new memslot set under SRCU through kvm_swap_active_memslots(). gfn_to_memslot() is the lookup every second-stage fault starts with, and hva_to_pfn() turns the resulting host address into a pfn through GUP or, for VM_PFNMAP memory, hva_to_pfn_remapped(). kvm_mmu_notifier_ops connects KVM to the host mm: kvm_mmu_notifier_invalidate_range_start() calls kvm_handle_hva_range() to zap second-stage entries before the host changes a page.
x86 TDP MMUarch/x86/kvm/mmu/mmu.c, arch/x86/kvm/mmu/tdp_mmu.ckvm_mmu_page_fault() and kvm_tdp_page_fault() resolve EPT/NPT violations. kvm_mmu_faultin_pfn() handles private/shared mismatches, invalid slots, and async page faults before the lock is taken. kvm_mmu_max_mapping_level() chooses the page size, and the NX huge page recovery worker undoes iTLB-multihit splits. kvm_tdp_mmu_map() installs the entry, and kvm_tdp_mmu_unmap_gfn_range() serves MMU-notifier invalidations.
x86 in-kernel LAPICarch/x86/kvm/lapic.ckvm_apic_set_irq() and __apic_accept_irq() deliver virtual interrupts, choosing posted delivery when APICv or AVIC is active.
x86 clocks and steal timearch/x86/kvm/x86.c, arch/x86/kvm/lapic.ckvm_guest_time_update() refreshes a vCPU's kvm-clock page, kvm_synchronize_tsc() matches TSC writes across vCPUs, and record_steal_time() updates the steal-time record and performs any TLB flush the guest queued for a preempted vCPU. In lapic.c, start_hv_timer() and start_sw_timer() choose between the VMX preemption timer and an hrtimer.
x86 posted interrupts and AVICarch/x86/kvm/vmx/posted_intr.c, arch/x86/kvm/svm/avic.c, virt/lib/irqbypass.cvmx_vcpu_pi_load() and vmx_vcpu_pi_put() retarget the posted-interrupt descriptor as a vCPU moves or blocks, and pi_wakeup_handler() wakes blocked vCPUs. vmx_pi_update_irte() and avic_pi_update_irte() program the IOMMU to post device interrupts, and avic_ga_log_notifier() wakes an AVIC vCPU that was not running. kvm_pi_update_irte() in arch/x86/kvm/irq.c decides whether an interrupt can be posted at all. irq_bypass_register_producer() and irq_bypass_register_consumer() pair a VFIO interrupt with a KVM irqfd.
ARM64 run loop and exitsarch/arm64/kvm/arm.c, arch/arm64/kvm/handle_exit.ckvm_arch_vcpu_ioctl_run() services requests in check_vcpu_requests(), flushes PMU and vGIC state, rechecks for late work in kvm_vcpu_exit_request(), enters the guest, and syncs PMU, vGIC, and timer state on the way out. handle_exit_early() deals with SErrors first. handle_exit() and handle_trap_exceptions() route each exit through arm_exit_handlers[] by ESR exception class.
ARM64 EL2 modes and host hypercallsarch/arm64/kvm/arm.c, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/hyp-main.c, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/hyp-entry.S, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/entry.Searly_kvm_mode_cfg() parses kvm-arm.mode, and init_hyp_mode() installs the nVHE object at EL2. In hyp-main.c, handle_trap() receives each host HVC and handle_host_hcall() dispatches it through host_hcall[], including handle___kvm_vcpu_run(). __kvm_hyp_vector is the EL2 vector table, and __guest_enter/__guest_exit switch the general-purpose registers.
ARM64 world switcharch/arm64/kvm/hyp/vhe/switch.c, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/switch.cThe two __kvm_vcpu_run() implementations save host state, load guest system registers, and enter the guest, from the kernel at EL2 (VHE) or from the separate EL2 object (nVHE, pKVM).
ARM64 EL2 fast exit handlersarch/arm64/kvm/hyp/include/hyp/switch.h, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/sys_regs.cfixup_guest_exit() and __fixup_guest_exit() try to finish an exit at EL2 through hyp_exit_handlers[] or, for protected guests, pvm_exit_handlers[]: kvm_hyp_handle_fpsimd() for lazy FP, kvm_hyp_handle_sysreg() and kvm_handle_cntxct() for register and counter traps, kvm_hyp_handle_mops(), and kvm_hyp_handle_memory_fault(). kvm_handle_pvm_sysreg() emulates a protected guest’s system registers from pvm_sys_reg_descs[].
Nested virtualizationarch/x86/kvm/vmx/nested.c, arch/x86/kvm/svm/nested.c, arch/arm64/kvm/nested.c, arch/arm64/kvm/emulate-nested.cnested_vmx_run() builds vmcs02 with prepare_vmcs02(), and nested_vmx_reflect_vmexit() decides whether L1 or L0 handles an L2 exit. nested_svm_vmrun() and nested_svm_vmexit() do the same for SVM. On ARM64, triage_sysreg_trap() decides whether a trapped register access goes to the guest hypervisor, kvm_inject_nested_sync() and kvm_emulate_nested_eret() move the vCPU between virtual EL2 and EL1, and kvm_vcpu_load_hw_mmu() picks the shadow stage 2 for L2.
ARM64 stage 2arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/pgtable.ckvm_handle_guest_abort() classifies a stage-2 fault, and user_mem_abort() chooses the mapping size and memory type and maps the page through kvm_pgtable_stage2_map(). gmem_abort() is the guest_memfd variant. Under pKVM, KVM_PGT_FN() routes the same calls to arch/arm64/kvm/pkvm.c, which asks EL2 to install the mapping.
ARM64 MMIO exitsarch/arm64/kvm/mmio.cio_mem_abort() decodes a data abort on an IPA with no memslot from the ESR syndrome, offers it to in-kernel devices through kvm_io_bus_write() and kvm_io_bus_read(), and otherwise fills run->mmio for a KVM_EXIT_MMIO. kvm_handle_mmio_return() completes a load on the next KVM_RUN. Since 7.0, LD64B/ST64B accesses leave as KVM_EXIT_ARM_LDST64B.
Protected KVMarch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/mem_protect.cThe EL2 page-ownership state machine: __pkvm_host_share_hyp() and __pkvm_host_share_guest() show how the host's own stage-2 is changed under hypervisor control.
pKVM VM and vCPU lifecyclearch/arm64/kvm/pkvm.c, arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/pkvm.cpkvm_create_hyp_vm() donates memory and asks EL2 to create its own copy of a VM, which __pkvm_init_vm() and __pkvm_init_vcpu() build from the host's struct kvm. pkvm_load_hyp_vcpu() runs at each vCPU load, and __pkvm_teardown_vm() reclaims the memory when the VM is destroyed.
ARM64 vGIC and timersarch/arm64/kvm/vgic/vgic.c, arch/arm64/kvm/arch_timer.ckvm_vgic_inject_irq() queues a virtual interrupt and vgic_flush_lr_state() loads list registers before entry, and vgic_v3_configure_hcr() in vgic-v3.c arms the maintenance interrupts that handle LR overflow. get_timer_map() decides which timers are direct and which emulated, kvm_timer_vcpu_load() hands the direct ones to the hardware, and kvm_vm_ioctl_set_counter_offset() implements KVM_ARM_SET_COUNTER_OFFSET.
GICv4 direct injectionarch/arm64/kvm/vgic/vgic-v4.cvgic_v4_init() allocates a vPE for each vCPU, kvm_vgic_v4_set_forwarding() maps a VFIO MSI to a vLPI when irqbypass pairs them, and vgic_v4_load() makes the vPE resident on the physical CPU at vCPU load.
guest_memfdvirt/kvm/guest_memfd.ckvm_gmem_create() builds the file behind KVM_CREATE_GUEST_MEMFD, and kvm_gmem_get_pfn() is how the fault paths reach private memory without a host virtual address. kvm_gmem_bind() ties a memslot to a range of the file, kvm_gmem_punch_hole() and kvm_gmem_release() show the invalidation that replaces MMU notifiers, and kvm_gmem_populate() loads a confidential guest's initial image.
Memory attributes and conversionvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, arch/x86/kvm/mmu/mmu.ckvm_vm_ioctl_set_mem_attributes() and kvm_vm_set_mem_attributes() record which gfns are private, with kvm_arch_pre_set_memory_attributes() and kvm_arch_post_set_memory_attributes() zapping and re-checking huge mappings around the change. kvm_mmu_prepare_memory_fault_exit() turns a private/shared mismatch into KVM_EXIT_MEMORY_FAULT.
SEV-SNP RMP and TDX module callsarch/x86/virt/svm/sev.c, arch/x86/virt/vmx/tdx/tdx.csnp_rmptable_init() sets up the Reverse Map Table, and rmp_make_private() and rmp_make_shared() change a page's RMP entry. tdx_enable() initializes the TDX module, and the SEAMCALL wrappers such as tdh_mng_create(), tdh_mem_page_aug(), and tdh_vp_enter() are how KVM acts on a trust domain.
irqfd and ioeventfdvirt/kvm/eventfd.ckvm_irqfd_assign() hooks an eventfd's wait queue to interrupt injection. kvm_irqfd_register() claims the queue as its exclusive priority waiter, and irqfd_wakeup() injects in the signaller's context. kvm_assign_ioeventfd() registers a guest address whose write signals an eventfd instead of exiting.
Halt polling and directed yieldvirt/kvm/kvm_main.c, drivers/cpuidle/cpuidle-haltpoll.ckvm_vcpu_halt() polls for a wake-up event before kvm_vcpu_block() sleeps, growing or shrinking halt_poll_ns. kvm_vcpu_on_spin() yields to a preempted vCPU of the same VM after a pause-loop or WFE exit. The cpuidle driver lets a guest poll in its own idle loop instead.
x86 virtual PMUarch/x86/kvm/pmu.c, kernel/events/core.cpmc_reprogram_counter() backs an emulated guest counter with a perf event, and kvm_pmu_deliver_pmi() injects the overflow. For the mediated vPMU (7.0), kvm_mediated_pmu_load() loads the guest's counters around each entry after perf_load_guest_context() parks host events, and perf_create_mediated_pmu() enforces exclusivity. kvm_vm_ioctl_set_pmu_event_filter() implements KVM_SET_PMU_EVENT_FILTER.
ARM64 PMU emulationarch/arm64/kvm/pmu-emul.c, arch/arm64/kvm/pmu.ckvm_pmu_create_perf_event() turns a guest event-type write into a perf event that excludes the host, and kvm_pmu_perf_overflow() raises the guest's PMU interrupt. kvm_arm_pmu_v3_enable() checks the configuration at first run. kvm_set_pmu_events() and kvm_vcpu_pmu_restore_guest() switch host counters that must not count in the guest.
Dirty trackingvirt/kvm/dirty_ring.c, virt/kvm/kvm_main.ckvm_dirty_ring_push() records each newly dirtied guest page in a per-vCPU ring that the VMM harvests during live migration. kvm_get_dirty_log_protect() and kvm_clear_dirty_log_protect() implement the older bitmap interface, and mark_page_dirty_in_slot() chooses between the two. On x86, kvm_mmu_slot_apply_flags() splits and write-protects a slot when logging starts, and vmx_flush_pml_buffer() drains Intel's PML buffer. Post-copy migration needs no KVM code: a vCPU fault on a page that has not arrived reaches handle_userfault() in fs/userfaultfd.c.
Asynchronous page faultsvirt/kvm/async_pf.ckvm_setup_async_pf() queues work that faults in a swapped-out page from a workqueue (async_pf_execute()) while the guest runs something else, and kvm_check_async_pf_completion() delivers the "page ready" notification.
In-kernel device modelsvirt/kvm/coalesced_mmio.c, arch/x86/kvm/i8254.c, arch/x86/kvm/hyperv.c, arch/x86/kvm/xen.ckvm_io_bus_register_dev() in virt/kvm/kvm_main.c inserts a device into a VM's sorted I/O bus by copy and RCU publication. coalesced_mmio_write() buffers MMIO writes in the shared ring, pit_do_work() injects PIT ticks with optional reinjection, and kvm_hv_hypercall() and kvm_xen_hypercall() complete Hyper-V and Xen hypercalls in the kernel or exit to the VMM.
