vhost-netdrivers/vhost/vhost.c, drivers/vhost/net.chandle_tx() and handle_rx() move packets between virtqueues and a tap socket using vhost_get_vq_desc() and vhost_add_used_and_signal(), and vhost_net_build_xdp() batches TX packets for tun. vhost_run_work_list() is the worker loop. vhost_log_write() records pages dirtied for migration, and vhost_iotlb_miss() asks the VMM for a device-IOTLB translation.
vhost worker threadskernel/vhost_task.cvhost_task_create() starts a vhost worker as a thread of the VMM process rather than a kernel thread, so it is accounted to the VMM's cgroups. vhost_task_fn() runs the worker function, and vhost_task_wake() wakes it.
VFIO and iommufddrivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_core.c, drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_intrs.c, drivers/vfio/vfio_main.c, drivers/iommu/iommufd/main.cvfio_pci_core_ioctl() and vfio_pci_core_mmap() expose a PCI device's regions and interrupts to the VMM, and vfio_pci_zap_bars() revokes the BAR mappings before a reset. vfio_intx_handler() masks a level-triggered INTx line until the guest's EOI unmasks it. vfio_mig_get_next_state() walks the migration state machine. iommufd owns the IOMMU page tables that confine the device's DMA.
KVM-VFIO devicevirt/kvm/vfio.ckvm_vfio_file_add() handles KVM_DEV_VFIO_FILE_ADD, kvm_vfio_file_set_kvm() gives the VFIO file its struct kvm, and kvm_vfio_update_coherency() registers non-coherent DMA so that x86 KVM honors guest memory types.
Linux as a KVM guestarch/x86/kernel/kvm.c, arch/arm64/kernel/paravirt.ckvm_guest_init() installs steal time, PV TLB flush, and PV spinlock hooks on x86, and kvm_smp_send_call_func_ipi() yields to a preempted IPI target with KVM_HC_SCHED_YIELD. The ARM64 file registers PV-time steal accounting.
Guest-side driversdrivers/firmware/smccc/kvm_guest.c, drivers/ptp/ptp_kvm_arm.c, drivers/virt/coco/pkvm-guest/arm-pkvm-guest.ckvm_init_hyp_services() discovers KVM's vendor hypercalls through SMCCC on ARM64, and kvm_arch_ptp_get_crosststamp() reads the host clock for ptp_kvm. kvm_arm_target_impl_cpu_init() asks the VMM which CPU implementations the VM may run on, so that their errata are handled. In a pKVM protected guest, pkvm_init_hyp_services() registers hooks that share and unshare pages with the host through hypercalls, and mmio_guard_ioremap_hook() tells the hypervisor which addresses are MMIO.
virtio in a KVM guestdrivers/virtio/virtio_ring.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_balloon.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_mem.c, mm/page_reporting.cvring_use_map_api() decides whether virtqueue buffers go through the DMA API, which a confidential guest requires. page_reporting_register() lets virtio-balloon report free pages to the host through virtballoon_free_page_report(), and virtio_mem_send_plug_request() asks the host to plug memory into a virtio-mem device.
Other hypervisor interfaces and the selftest VMMdrivers/hv/mshv_root_main.c, drivers/virt/acrn/hsm.c, drivers/virt/nitro_enclaves/ne_misc_dev.c, tools/testing/selftests/kvm/lib/kvm_util.cmshv_dev_ioctl() and mshv_ioctl_create_partition() implement /dev/mshv, and mshv_run_vp_with_root_scheduler() calls xfer_to_guest_mode_handle_work() as KVM does. acrn_dev_ioctl() and ne_create_vm_ioctl() are the ACRN and Nitro Enclaves entry points. In the selftests, __vm_create(), vm_userspace_mem_region_add(), and __vm_vcpu_add() form a minimal VMM.
virtio coredrivers/virtio/virtio.c, include/linux/virtio.h, include/linux/virtio_config.h, drivers/virtio/virtio_debug.cvirtio_dev_probe() resets the status, negotiates features with virtio_features_ok(), and calls the driver's probe(), which must set DRIVER_OK before using its queues. register_virtio_device() is what every transport calls to put a device on the virtio bus, __register_virtio_driver() adds a driver, virtio_config_changed() delivers configuration-change interrupts, and virtio_debug_device_init() creates the per-device debugfs feature filter.
Virtqueuesdrivers/virtio/virtio_ring.c, include/uapi/linux/virtio_ring.hvring_create_virtqueue() allocates a split or packed ring. Drivers post buffers with virtqueue_add_sgs(), decide whether to notify with virtqueue_kick_prepare() (the event-suppression check), and reap completions with virtqueue_get_buf(). vring_interrupt() is the used-buffer interrupt handler, virtqueue_enable_cb_delayed() re-arms callbacks with a threshold, and virtqueue_resize() changes a ring's size through VIRTIO_F_RING_RESET. struct vring_desc_extra shadows each descriptor's DMA metadata and holds the free list, virtqueue_use_indirect() decides when a chain gets an indirect table, BAD_RING() breaks a queue on a bad used ID, and virtqueue_map_single_attrs() (6.18 name) maps premapped buffers.
virtio-pcidrivers/virtio/virtio_pci_common.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_pci_modern.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_pci_modern_dev.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_pci_legacy.c, include/uapi/linux/virtio_pci.hvirtio_pci_probe() tries the modern interface (virtio_pci_modern_probe(), which finds the vendor capabilities in vp_modern_probe()) before legacy. vp_find_vqs() assigns MSI-X vectors per queue or shared, setup_vq() programs the common configuration structure, vp_notify() writes the queue index to the notify address, and vp_vring_interrupt() handles a shared or INTx interrupt. vp_check_common_size() refuses a feature whose common-configuration field the device did not provide, and vp_find_vqs() tries the per-queue, shared-slow, and shared MSI-X policies before INTx.
Admin virtqueue and vfio-virtiodrivers/virtio/virtio_pci_modern.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_pci_admin_legacy_io.c, drivers/vfio/pci/virtio/main.cvp_modern_admin_cmd_exec() sends a command on the PF's admin virtqueue on behalf of a member VF. virtio_pci_admin_legacy_common_io_write() and virtio_pci_admin_legacy_io_notify_info() let virtiovf_pci_probe()'s vfio driver present a modern VF to a guest as a legacy device and support live migration.
virtio-mmiodrivers/virtio/virtio_mmio.c, include/uapi/linux/virtio_mmio.h, Documentation/devicetree/bindings/virtio/virtio_mmio_probe() binds a virtio,mmio device-tree node, ACPI LNRO0005 device, or command-line device and checks the magic value and version. vm_setup_vq() programs the queue registers, vm_notify() writes QueueNotify, and vm_interrupt() reads and acknowledges InterruptStatus.
Other virtio transportsdrivers/s390/virtio/virtio_ccw.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_vdpa.c, drivers/remoteproc/remoteproc_virtio.c, arch/um/drivers/virtio_uml.cvirtio_ccw_online() brings up a virtio device over s390 channel I/O, virtio_vdpa_probe() turns a vDPA device into a virtio device for in-kernel drivers, rproc_add_virtio_dev() creates virtio devices from a remote processor's resource table with rproc_virtio_notify() as the doorbell, and virtio_uml_probe() connects User-Mode Linux to a vhost-user device.
Writing a virtio driverdrivers/char/hw_random/virtio-rng.c, Documentation/driver-api/virtio/writing_virtio_drivers.rst, include/uapi/linux/virtio_ids.hA small complete driver: probe_common() finds one virtqueue and calls virtio_device_ready(), and random_recv_done() is the virtqueue callback that collects the filled buffer. The documentation walks through the same skeleton, and virtio_ids.h holds the device-type numbers that the spec assigns. For the request path, virtio_queue_rq() in drivers/block/virtio_blk.c stops the hardware queue on -ENOSPC and kicks only for the request marked last, virtio_commit_rqs() kicks when blk-mq ends a batch early, and virtblk_done() restarts stopped queues; drivers/gpio/gpio-virtio.c shows __dma_from_device_group_begin() (include/linux/dma-mapping.h) separating device-written fields from CPU-written ones.
vhost backend APIdrivers/vhost/vhost.c, drivers/vhost/test.cvhost_dev_init() and vhost_dev_ioctl()/vhost_vring_ioctl() implement the generic vhost ioctls, vhost_vq_init_access() validates a ring before use, vhost_get_vq_desc() fetches the next descriptor chain as iovecs, vhost_add_used_and_signal() completes it and signals the guest through the call eventfd, vhost_enable_notify() re-enables kicks with a race check, and vhost_poll_queue() schedules the worker. handle_vq() in test.c is the minimal loop built on them.
vDPA and VDUSEdrivers/vdpa/vdpa.c, include/linux/vdpa.h, drivers/vhost/vdpa.c, drivers/vdpa/vdpa_user/vduse_dev.c, include/uapi/linux/vduse.h__vdpa_alloc_device() and _vdpa_register_device() add a device whose data path follows the virtio ring layout and that implements struct vdpa_config_ops. vhost_vdpa_probe() exposes it to a VMM as /dev/vhost-vdpa-N, and vhost_vdpa_fault() maps its doorbell page into the VMM. VDUSE puts the device in a user-space process: vduse_create_dev() creates it, vduse_dev_msg_sync() forwards control-path requests to the process, vduse_vdpa_kick_vq() signals its kick eventfd, and vduse_dev_queue_irq_work() injects its interrupts. vdpa_mgmtdev_register() exposes a parent to the vdpa netlink tool, and suspend(), get_vq_state(), and set_group_asid() in struct vdpa_config_ops support live migration and control-queue shadowing.
vringh and virtio testingdrivers/vhost/vringh.c, tools/virtio/vringh_init_kern(), vringh_getdesc_kern(), and vringh_complete_kern() implement the device side of a split ring in kernel memory, as the vDPA simulators, the mlx5 vDPA control virtqueue, and caif_virtio use it, while vringh_init_iotlb() handles rings reached through an IOTLB. tools/virtio/virtio_test.c, vringh_test.c, and ringtest/ exercise the ring code in user space against drivers/vhost/test.c. tools/virtio/vhost_net_test.c drives vhost-net through a tap device.
virtio memory-access policy and dma-buf sharingdrivers/virtio/virtio_anchor.c, drivers/virtio/virtio_dma_buf.c, drivers/xen/grant-dma-ops.cvirtio_set_mem_acc_cb() installs the architecture's rule for when a device must use ACCESS_PLATFORM, xen_virtio_restricted_mem_acc() switches a Xen guest's virtio device to grant-table DMA, and virtio_dma_buf_export()/virtio_dma_buf_get_uuid() let virtio devices name a shared host object by UUID.
virtio shared memory regionsinclude/linux/virtio_config.h, drivers/virtio/virtio_mmio.c, fs/fuse/virtio_fs.c, drivers/nvdimm/virtio_pmem.cvirtio_get_shm_region() asks the transport for a device-owned memory region by ID; vm_get_shm_region() reads the MMIO SHMSel registers, virtio-fs maps its DAX window from one, and virtio_pmem_validate() drops VIRTIO_PMEM_F_SHMEM_REGION when it is missing.

Chapter 29 — Tracing, Debugging, and Kernel Diagnostics

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
Who may read diagnosticskernel/printk/printk.c, kernel/ksyms_common.c, kernel/events/core.c, include/linux/security.h, security/lockdown/lockdown.ccheck_syslog_permissions() applies dmesg_restrict; kallsyms_show_value() combines kptr_restrict, perf_event_paranoid and CAP_SYSLOG; perf_allow_kernel() is the paranoid-level test for kernel profiling; enum lockdown_reason lists every interface lockdown can close, with LOCKDOWN_INTEGRITY_MAX and LOCKDOWN_CONFIDENTIALITY_MAX marking the two levels.
noinstr and recursion guardsinclude/linux/compiler_types.h, include/linux/trace_recursion.h, kernel/trace/ring_buffer.c, kernel/events/core.c, kernel/kprobes.cThe __noinstr_section() macro lists every kind of instrumentation that noinstr switches off; populate_kprobe_blacklist() keeps kprobes out of .noinstr.text; ftrace_test_recursion_trylock(), trace_recursive_lock() and perf_swevent_get_recursion_context() are the three per-context recursion guards.
printk and the log ring bufferkernel/printk/printk.c, kernel/printk/printk_ringbuffer.c, kernel/printk/printk_ringbuffer.h, lib/vsprintf.cStart with vprintk_emit() and vprintk_store(), then read the long comment at the top of kernel/printk/printk_ringbuffer.c and follow prb_reserve(), prb_commit() and prb_final_commit() to see how the descriptor and text rings stay lockless. setup_log_buf() moves the log from the static to the dynamic buffer, and pointer() in lib/vsprintf.c dispatches the %p extensions and hashing.
/dev/kmsg, syslog(2), dynamic debug and the printk indexkernel/printk/printk.c, lib/dynamic_debug.c, include/linux/dynamic_debug.h, kernel/printk/index.c, drivers/base/core.cdevkmsg_read() and do_syslog() show the two readers and the dmesg_restrict check; struct _ddebug and ddebug_exec_query() show how pr_debug() sites are switched on at run time. info_print_ext_header(), devkmsg_llseek() and devkmsg_write() define the /dev/kmsg record format, cursor and injection rules. pi_show() prints one line of the debugfs printk index per call site, and dev_err_probe() with __dev_probe_failed() shows how a deferred probe is logged quietly and recorded as the deferral reason.
Consoles and nbconkernel/printk/nbcon.c, include/linux/console.hstruct nbcon_state and nbcon_context_try_acquire() define the ownership protocol; nbcon_atomic_flush_pending() and nbcon_kthread_func() are the atomic and threaded printing paths. register_console() and get_init_console_seq() in kernel/printk/printk.c handle console=, boot consoles and replay; nbcon_device_try_acquire() is how uart_port_lock() joins the protocol.
BUG(), WARN() and the bug tablelib/bug.c, include/asm-generic/bug.h, arch/x86/include/asm/bug.h, arch/arm64/kernel/debug-monitors.c, kernel/panic.c (__warn, check_panic_on_warn, clear_warn_once)__report_bug() and find_bug() are shared; compare x86 handle_bug() in arch/x86/kernel/traps.c with ARM64 call_el1_break_hook() to see UD2 versus BRK dispatch. __warn() prints the report and applies panic_on_warn and warn_limit; Documentation/process/deprecated.rst explains why new BUG_ON() calls are refused.
Oops, taint and panickernel/panic.c, arch/x86/kernel/dumpstack.c, arch/x86/mm/fault.c, arch/arm64/kernel/traps.c, arch/arm64/mm/fault.c, kernel/exit.c, lib/dump_stack.c, lib/sys_info.cRead vpanic() top to bottom for the order of kdump, notifiers and kmsg_dump(); add_taint() and taint_flags[] define the taint letters; oops_begin()/oops_end() and ARM64 die() show the oops path. show_fault_oops() and die_kernel_fault() print the page-fault preamble, dump_stack_print_info() the CPU: line, make_task_dead() applies oops_limit, and sys_info() implements panic_sys_info=.
pstore, kmsg_dump and SysRqfs/pstore/platform.c, fs/pstore/ram.c, fs/pstore/zone.c, include/linux/kmsg_dump.h, kernel/printk/printk.c, drivers/gpu/drm/drm_panic.c, drivers/tty/sysrq.ckmsg_dump_desc() calls the registered dumpers; pstore_dump() and ramoops_pstore_write() save the log; drm_panic() is a dumper that draws on the screen; sysrq_key_table[] and __handle_sysrq() list and dispatch the SysRq commands, and write_sysrq_trigger() handles the _ bulk mode.
Stack unwindersarch/x86/kernel/unwind_orc.c, arch/x86/kernel/dumpstack.c, arch/arm64/kernel/stacktrace.c, kernel/stacktrace.corc_find() and unwind_next_frame() apply the objtool-generated ORC entries; __show_trace_log_lvl() scans each x86 stack and marks unconfirmed addresses with ?; kunwind_stack_walk(), kunwind_next_frame_record() and kunwind_next_regs_pc() follow ARM64 frame records across exception boundaries; stack_trace_save_tsk_reliable() is what livepatch relies on.
kallsyms and symbolized addresseskernel/kallsyms.c, scripts/kallsyms.c, scripts/link-vmlinux.sh, lib/vsprintf.cget_symbol_pos() and kallsyms_expand_symbol() search and decompress the table; kallsyms_lookup_buildid() falls back to modules, BPF and ftrace trampolines; __sprint_symbol() formats func+off/size [mod] for %pS and %pB; optimize_token_table() in scripts/kallsyms.c builds the 256-token compression table.
Stack depotlib/stackdepot.c, include/linux/stackdepot.hstack_depot_save_flags() hashes a trace and stores it once in a pool, returning a 32-bit handle; stack_depot_put() drops the reference taken with STACK_DEPOT_FLAG_GET; mm/page_owner.c is a short example of a caller.
Lockup, hung-task and workqueue watchdogskernel/watchdog.c, kernel/watchdog_perf.c, kernel/watchdog_buddy.c, arch/arm64/kernel/watchdog_hld.c, arch/x86/kernel/apic/hw_nmi.c, kernel/hung_task.c, kernel/workqueue.c, lib/Kconfig.debugwatchdog_timer_fn() drives both lockup checks: read is_softlockup() for the period and SOFTLOCKUP_DELAY_REPORT logic, report_cpu_status() for the interrupt-storm tables, and watchdog_hardlockup_check() for the hard-lockup decision and report. Compare the NMI-based watchdog_overflow_callback() and its watchdog_check_timestamp() turbo filter with watchdog_buddy_check_hardlockup(); hw_nmi_get_sample_period() exists once per architecture, and lockup_detector_retry_init() is how a late PMU driver gets probed. The watchdog_sysctls table lists every knob. In kernel/hung_task.c, task_is_hung() is the whole test, check_hung_task() the report and panic threshold, and debug_show_blocker() the lock-owner lookup. wq_watchdog_timer_fn() and panic_on_wq_watchdog() in kernel/workqueue.c are the workqueue stall check; the HARDLOCKUP_DETECTOR* entries in lib/Kconfig.debug show how perf, buddy and arch detectors are chosen.
Debug objects, kmemleak, fault injection, UBSAN and VM assertionslib/debugobjects.c, mm/kmemleak.c, lib/fault-inject.c, kernel/fail_function.c, lib/ubsan.c, lib/Kconfig.ubsan, include/linux/mmdebug.h, kernel/configs/debug.configdebug_object_activate() and debug_print_object() check object life cycles, and __debug_check_no_obj_freed() catches an active object freed with its memory; the timer descriptor and timer_fixup_free() in kernel/time/timer.c show a fixup. kmemleak_scan(), scan_block() and update_checksum() are the conservative leak scan, print_unreferenced() the report, and kmemleak_write() the debugfs commands. should_fail_ex() decides each injected failure, including the fail_nth countdown, and fei_kprobe_handler() overrides a function's return value. ubsan_prologue() starts every UBSAN report and report_ubsan_failure() decodes a trap. The VM_BUG_ON_*() macros show what CONFIG_DEBUG_VM compiles in and out, and debug.config is the ready-made debug configuration.
ftrace call sites and code patchingkernel/trace/ftrace.c, kernel/trace/Kconfig, scripts/sorttable.c, arch/x86/kernel/ftrace.c, arch/x86/kernel/ftrace_64.S, arch/arm64/kernel/ftrace.c, arch/arm64/kernel/entry-ftrace.Sftrace_process_locs() builds the dyn_ftrace records from __mcount_loc and ftrace_update_code() NOPs them at boot; test_for_valid_rec() and sort_mcount_loc() deal with weak functions; ftrace_modify_all_code() orders a change and ftrace_bug() reports a failed one; compare x86 ftrace_replace_code() with ARM64 ftrace_make_call(), ftrace_make_nop() and ftrace_init_nop(), then read ftrace_caller in each assembly file.
Live kernel text patchingarch/x86/kernel/alternative.c, arch/arm64/kernel/patching.csmp_text_poke_batch_add() and smp_text_poke_batch_finish() run the x86 INT3 protocol while smp_text_poke_int3_handler() emulates the instruction being patched; ARM64 aarch64_insn_patch_text_nosync() writes one instruction through the FIX_TEXT_POKE0 fixmap and aarch64_insn_patch_text() uses stop_machine for the general case.
ftrace_ops and function_graphinclude/linux/ftrace.h, include/linux/trace_recursion.h, kernel/trace/fgraph.c, kernel/trace/trace_functions_graph.cstruct ftrace_ops and register_ftrace_function() are the client API; ftrace_ops_get_func() and ftrace_ops_assist_func() apply FTRACE_OPS_FL_RECURSION, and ftrace_shutdown() shows the RCU-tasks waits before a dynamic ops is freed; function_graph_enter_regs() and ftrace_return_to_handler() push and pop the shadow return stack, and fgraph_reserve_data() passes data from entry to return.
Live patching and direct callskernel/livepatch/patch.c, kernel/trace/ftrace.c, kernel/bpf/trampoline.cklp_ftrace_handler() redirects a patched function by rewriting the saved instruction pointer with ftrace_regs_set_instruction_pointer(); register_ftrace_direct() points a call site straight at a trampoline, as BPF does, and update_ftrace_direct_add() serves the single shared direct ops on x86-64; bpf_tramp_ftrace_ops_func() negotiates IPMODIFY sharing with live patching.
Trace ring buffer and tracefskernel/trace/ring_buffer.c, include/linux/ring_buffer.h, include/uapi/linux/trace_mmap.h, kernel/trace/trace.c, kernel/trace/trace_output.c, kernel/trace/trace_clock.cThe comment above RB_PAGE_HEAD explains the flag bits in the page list; enum ring_buffer_type defines the type_len encoding; __rb_reserve_next() and rb_get_reader_page() are the writer and reader sides; trace_recursive_lock() refuses same-context recursion; rb_wakeups() defers reader wakeups to irq_work and rb_watermark_hit() applies buffer_percent; struct trace_buffer_meta is the mmap meta page; tracer_init_tracefs() creates the control files; tracing_stats_read() prints the per-CPU loss counters; trace_print_lat_fmt() decodes the flag characters; trace_clocks[] lists the clocks.
trace_printk and trace_markerinclude/linux/trace_printk.h, kernel/trace/trace_printk.c, kernel/trace/trace.cThe do_trace_printk macro saves the format pointer and calls __trace_bprintk(); trace_printk_init_buffers() allocates the buffers and prints the NOTICE banner; tracing_mark_write() takes records written by user space.
Instances, snapshots and persistent bufferskernel/trace/trace.c, kernel/trace/ring_buffer.cinstance_mkdir() and trace_array_get_by_name() create instances, and enable_instances() parses trace_instance=; tracing_snapshot_write() and tracing_snapshot_instance() swap in the spare buffer; __ring_buffer_alloc_range() builds a buffer over reserved memory that survives a reboot, rb_cpu_meta_valid() checks it on the next boot, trace_adjust_address() relocates old addresses, update_last_data() discards the old data and backup_instance_area() copies it.

Hist triggers and synthetic eventskernel/trace/trace_events_hist.c, kernel/trace/trace_events_synth.c, kernel/trace/trace_events_trigger.c, kernel/trace/tracing_map.c, include/trace/trace_custom_events.h, samples/trace_events/trace_custom_sched.cevent_hist_trigger() updates the tracing map per event; onmatch handling and trace_synth() show how one event fires another; trace_trigger_soft_disabled() and __event_trigger_test_discard() are the two places triggers run; TRACE_CUSTOM_EVENT() gives an existing tracepoint a new record layout.
Event filters, eprobes and user_eventskernel/trace/trace_events_filter.c, kernel/trace/trace.c, kernel/trace/trace_eprobe.c, kernel/trace/trace_events_user.ccreate_filter() compiles a filter expression and filter_match_preds() runs it for each event; trace_event_buffer_lock_reserve() builds filtered events in the per-CPU trace_buffered_event page; eprobe_trigger_func() fires an eprobe from an existing event; user_events_ioctl() registers events from user space.
kprobes and kretprobeskernel/kprobes.c, arch/x86/kernel/kprobes/core.c, arch/x86/kernel/kprobes/opt.c, arch/arm64/kernel/probes/kprobes.c, kernel/trace/rethook.c, arch/x86/kernel/kprobes/ftrace.c, arch/arm64/kernel/probes/decode-insn.cregister_kprobe() and check_kprobe_address_safe() decide what can be probed; compare kprobe_int3_handler() with ARM64 kprobe_brk_handler(); rethook_hook() replaces return addresses for kretprobes. arch_adjust_kprobe_addr() steps over ENDBR64; kprobe_ftrace_handler() runs probes on ftrace sites; prepare_emulation() lists the x86 instructions emulated in the trap; can_optimize() in opt.c decides when an INT3 becomes a JMP; arm_kprobe_decode_insn() decides what ARM64 simulates, steps or rejects; register_kretprobe() sizes the instance pool; report_probe() formats the debugfs list.
fprobes and dynamic eventskernel/trace/fprobe.c, kernel/trace/trace_kprobe.c, kernel/trace/trace_fprobe.c, kernel/trace/trace_probe.c, Documentation/trace/kprobetrace.rst, Documentation/trace/fprobetrace.rstregister_fprobe() shows fprobes on the fgraph infrastructure; trace_kprobe_create() and traceprobe_parse_probe_arg() parse the kprobe_events and dynamic_events syntax. fprobe_fgraph_entry() reserves per-call entry data and counts misses; fprobe_ftrace_entry() is the entry-only path; fprobe_module_callback() drops a departing module's addresses; validate_probe_symbol() rejects ambiguous symbols; within_notrace_func() keeps kprobe events off untraceable functions.
uprobeskernel/events/uprobes.c, include/linux/uprobes.h, arch/x86/kernel/uprobes.c, arch/arm64/kernel/probes/uprobes.cuprobe_register(), install_breakpoint() and uprobe_write_opcode() place the breakpoint; handle_swbp() and xol_get_insn_slot() run the probe and the out-of-line step. valid_vma() says which mappings qualify; uprobe_write() and __uprobe_write() break copy-on-write and restore the page-cache page on removal; find_active_uprobe_speculative() looks up a hit without mmap_lock; handler_chain() applies UPROBE_HANDLER_REMOVE and UPROBE_HANDLER_IGNORE; on x86, sys_uretprobe, sys_uprobe and arch_uprobe_optimize() replace the traps with system calls.

Deferred user-space unwindingkernel/unwind/deferred.c, kernel/unwind/user.c, include/linux/unwind_deferred.h, kernel/events/callchain.c, arch/arm64/kernel/perf_callchain.cunwind_deferred_request() queues the task_work and process_unwind_deferred() runs unwind_user_faultable() once per kernel entry for every requester. perf_callchain() decides whether to defer, and get_perf_callchain() emits PERF_CONTEXT_USER_DEFERRED or calls perf_callchain_user().

Runtime verificationkernel/trace/rv/rv.c, kernel/trace/rv/rv_reactors.c, include/rv/da_monitor.h, include/rv/ltl_monitor.h, kernel/trace/rv/monitors/wip/wip.c, tools/verification/models/da_event() is the try_cmpxchg() transition loop behind da_handle_event(); rv_register_monitor() takes the parent container; rv_get_task_monitor_slot() hands out per-task slots; ltl_atom_update() drives an LTL monitor; rv_react() calls the reactor.

Appendix A — System Startup

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
UEFI firmware after the hand-off: memory map, runtime services, Secure Boot keysdrivers/firmware/efi/efi.c (efi_systab_report_header, efi_mm), drivers/firmware/efi/runtime-wrappers.c (efi_rts_wq), drivers/firmware/efi/memattr.c (efi_memattr_apply_permissions), drivers/firmware/efi/unaccepted_memory.c (accept_memory), arch/x86/platform/efi/efi.c (efi_enter_virtual_mode), arch/x86/platform/efi/quirks.c (efi_crash_gracefully_on_page_fault), drivers/firmware/efi/stmm/tee_stmm_efi.c, security/integrity/platform_certs/load_uefi.c (load_uefi_certs), fs/efivarfs/What firmware leaves behind and how the kernel lives with it: the typed memory map, including the unaccepted memory of confidential guests, accepted lazily since 6.5; runtime services mapped into a private efi_mm with permissions from the Memory Attributes Table and called only through one workqueue, so a firmware fault can be contained; and the db, dbx, and MOK lists loaded into the platform, machine, and blacklist keyrings so Secure Boot decisions outlive boot services.
ARM64 firmware interfaces: PSCI, SMCCC, and the DT/ACPI choicedrivers/firmware/psci/psci.c (psci_dt_init, psci_acpi_init), drivers/firmware/smccc/smccc.c (arm_smccc_version_init), arch/arm64/kernel/cpu_ops.c, arch/arm64/kernel/acpi.c (dt_is_stub), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/fdt.c (update_fdt), arch/arm64/include/asm/archrandom.h, drivers/firmware/arm_sdei.c, drivers/firmware/arm_scmi/, drivers/firmware/arm_ffa/, Documentation/arch/arm64/booting.rstHow an ARM64 kernel talks to the EL3 monitor and secure world that stay resident beneath it: PSCI to start, stop, and idle CPUs over SMC or, in a guest, HVC; the other SMCCC services (TRNG entropy, SDEI events, SCMI power control, FF-A); and how the stub-built /chosen node and dt_is_stub decide between device tree and ACPI.
The x86 boot protocol and bzImagearch/x86/include/uapi/asm/bootparam.h (struct setup_header, struct boot_params, XLF_*), arch/x86/boot/header.S (HdrS, PE/COFF header, sentinel), arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/setup_data.h (SETUP_* types), arch/x86/boot/compressed/kernel_info.S (kernel_info), arch/x86/include/asm/bootparam_utils.h (sanitize_boot_params), Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rstThe contract between a loader and the x86 kernel, now at protocol 2.15. The setup header at a fixed offset in the image is where a loader reads the protocol version and load flags and writes the command-line pointer, initrd location, and video mode; boot_params carries that and the firmware memory map into the kernel, setup_data records carry what does not fit, and the same bytes double as a PE/COFF header for UEFI. The document defines the 16-, 32-, and 64-bit entry protocols.
Real-mode setup (BIOS path)arch/x86/boot/main.c (main, set_bios_mode), arch/x86/boot/cpu.c (validate_cpu), arch/x86/boot/memory.c (detect_memory_e820), arch/x86/boot/edd.c (query_edd), arch/x86/boot/pm.c (go_to_protected_mode), arch/x86/boot/pmjump.S (protected_mode_jump), REALMODE_CFLAGS in arch/x86/MakefileThe successor to the 2.6.11 16-bit assembly setup code, rewritten in C for 2.6.23 and compiled with -m16, still there for legacy BIOS boots: it checks the CPU, asks the BIOS for the E820 memory map and disk parameters, enables A20, masks interrupts, and climbs into protected mode at the decompressor's startup_32. A UEFI boot never runs it.
The EFI stub, common partdrivers/firmware/efi/libstub/efi-stub-entry.c (efi_pe_entry), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/efi-stub.c (efi_stub_common), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/efi-stub-helper.c (efi_load_initrd, efi_load_initrd_cmdline, efi_exit_boot_services), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/fdt.c (efi_boot_kernel), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/mem.c, include/linux/pe.h (LINUX_EFISTUB_MAJOR_VERSION)How the kernel image becomes a UEFI application. The stub uses boot services to load the initrd (LoadFile2 first, then initrd=), fetch the memory map, and pass ACPI and device-tree pointers on, and efi_exit_boot_services is the point after which firmware can no longer help. The PE image version tells a loader which initrd methods the stub supports.
The x86 EFI stubdrivers/firmware/efi/libstub/x86-stub.c (efi_stub_entry, efi_handover_entry, efi_decompress_kernel, efi_adjust_memory_range_protection, exit_boot, enter_kernel), arch/x86/boot/startup/efi-mixed.S, arch/x86/boot/compressed/misc.c (decompress_kernel)Since Linux 6.6 the x86 stub decompresses the kernel itself into a randomly placed allocation and jumps straight to startup_64, skipping the legacy decompressor entirely; mixed mode lets a 64-bit kernel start from 32-bit firmware. efi_stub_entry is also a checklist of a modern loader's duties: Secure Boot state, memory protections, random seed, TPM log, framebuffer, PCI ROMs, and unaccepted memory.
The ARM64 Image header and EFI zbootarch/arm64/include/asm/image.h (ARM64_IMAGE_MAGIC, ARM64_IMAGE_FLAG_*), arch/arm64/kernel/head.S (image header), arch/arm64/Makefile (boot targets), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/arm64-stub.c (handle_kernel_image), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/zboot.c (efi_zboot_entry), scripts/make_fit.py, Documentation/arch/arm64/booting.rstARM64's whole boot protocol: a 64-byte header that doubles as a PE/COFF header, placement rules for the image, device tree, and initrd, a per-extension list of registers firmware must set, and a register contract (x0 holds the device tree). With CONFIG_EFI_ZBOOT the compressed vmlinuz.efi decompresses itself before running the ordinary stub; make image.fit packs the kernel with every device tree.
What travels down the chain: command line, bootconfig, boot seedinit/main.c (parse_early_param, unknown_bootoption, setup_boot_config), kernel/params.c (parse_args), lib/bootconfig.c, tools/bootconfig/, drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/tpm.c, drivers/firmware/efi/efi.c, drivers/of/fdt.c (rng-seed), security/integrity/ima/ima_efi.cHow the kernel reads its command line twice (early_param before memory management, __setup and module parameters later) and hands every unclaimed word to init; how bootconfig appended to the initrd adds kernel. and init. keys; and how the boot RNG seed is consumed and then wiped. The TPM log and IMA's Secure Boot policy carry the measured and verified chain into the running kernel.
The legacy x86 decompressorarch/x86/boot/compressed/Makefile (build pipeline), arch/x86/tools/relocs.c, arch/x86/boot/compressed/mkpiggy.c, arch/x86/boot/compressed/head_64.S, arch/x86/boot/compressed/pgtable_64.c (configure_5level_paging), arch/x86/boot/compressed/ident_map_64.c (initialize_identity_maps, do_boot_page_fault), arch/x86/boot/compressed/misc.c (extract_kernel, parse_elf, handle_relocations), arch/x86/boot/header.S (init_size), lib/decompress_inflate.c, arch/arm64/kernel/pi/relocate.cThe path a BIOS or non-EFI loader still takes: the compressed kernel moves itself to the end of its buffer so it can be inflated in place, maps memory on demand from a page-fault handler, picks a random location, decompresses, and applies relocations from a table appended at build time before jumping to the real kernel. The Makefile header comment documents the VO/ZO pipeline; ARM64 applies its RELA/RELR relocations in its own early code instead.
KASLRarch/x86/boot/compressed/kaslr.c (choose_random_location, mem_avoid_init, find_random_phys_addr, find_random_virt_addr), arch/x86/lib/kaslr.c (kaslr_get_random_long), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/x86-stub.c (efi_decompress_kernel), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/kaslr.c (efi_kaslr_get_phys_seed), drivers/firmware/efi/libstub/randomalloc.c (efi_random_alloc), arch/arm64/kernel/pi/kaslr_early.c (kaslr_early_init), arch/arm64/kernel/pi/map_kernel.c, arch/arm64/kernel/kaslr.c (kaslr_init), RANDOMIZE_BASE help in arch/x86/KconfigWhere each half of the image randomization is decided. The physical address comes from the decompressor or the EFI stub, after avoiding the initrd, command line, memmap= regions, and 1 GiB huge pages; ARM64's virtual offset is picked by the kernel itself from /chosen/kaslr-seed or RNDR. The x86 Kconfig help states the ranges and entropy.
Randomization beyond the kernel imagearch/x86/mm/kaslr.c (kernel_randomize_memory, kaslr_regions), arch/x86/mm/init.c (execmem_arch_setup), arch/x86/mm/cpu_entry_area.c (init_cea_offsets), arch/arm64/mm/init.c (module_init_limits), include/linux/randomize_kstack.h, lib/vsprintf.c (kptr_restrict, ptr_to_id), kernel/ksyms_common.c (kallsyms_show_value), arch/arm64/include/asm/mmu.h (kaslr_requires_kpti), arch/x86/kernel/setup.c (dump_kernel_offset)The randomization that follows the image: x86 shuffles the direct map, vmalloc, and vmemmap bases, both architectures randomize module placement and per-syscall stack offsets, and x86 randomizes each CPU's entry area. The rest shows how the kernel keeps the offset secret—pointer hashing, kptr_restrict, KPTI or E0PD—and how it still reports it on a panic.
x86_64 entry to start_kernelarch/x86/kernel/head_64.S (startup_64, secondary_startup_64, common_startup_64), arch/x86/kernel/verify_cpu.S, arch/x86/boot/startup/map_kernel.c (__startup_64, check_la57_support), arch/x86/boot/startup/gdt_idt.c, arch/x86/boot/startup/sme.c (sme_enable), arch/x86/boot/startup/Makefile, arch/x86/kernel/head64.c (x86_64_start_kernel, x86_64_start_reservations)The convergence point of every x86 boot path. The __pi_-prefixed code under arch/x86/boot/startup/ (moved there in 6.16) runs before the kernel is at its link-time address and fixes up the early page tables for wherever it actually landed; common_startup_64 is shared with every secondary CPU, and x86_64_start_kernel shows the last steps before start_kernel.
ARM64 entry to start_kernelarch/arm64/kernel/head.S (primary_entry, record_mmu_state, init_kernel_el, __primary_switch, __primary_switched), arch/arm64/mm/proc.S (__cpu_setup), arch/arm64/kernel/pi/map_range.c (create_init_idmap), arch/arm64/kernel/pi/map_kernel.c (early_map_kernel, map_kernel), arch/arm64/kernel/pi/idreg-override.c, arch/arm64/kernel/pi/patch-scs.c, arch/arm64/kernel/pi/Makefile, arch/arm64/kernel/hyp-stub.S (__hyp_stub_vectors, finalise_el2), arch/arm64/kernel/setup.c (__fdt_pointer)The exception-level descent and the MMU switch-on. The kernel always drops to EL1 first, maps and relocates itself from position-independent C code—applying command-line CPU-feature overrides, the VA size, KASLR, and the two-pass text mapping—and only then, with VHE, returns to EL2.
Secondary CPU bring-uparch/x86/realmode/rm/trampoline_64.S, arch/x86/realmode/init.c (reserve_real_mode), arch/x86/kernel/smpboot.c (do_boot_cpu, arch_cpuhp_init_parallel_bringup, start_secondary), arch/x86/kernel/acpi/madt_wakeup.c, arch/arm64/kernel/smp.c (__cpu_up, secondary_start_kernel), arch/arm64/kernel/psci.c, arch/arm64/kernel/smp_spin_table.c, arch/arm64/kernel/acpi_parking_protocol.c, arch/arm64/kernel/head.S (secondary_entry, secondary_holding_pen, __secondary_switched)Why x86 application processors still start in 16-bit real mode below 1 MB, how they have been started in parallel since 6.5, and how confidential guests skip real mode; and how ARM64 instead asks firmware through PSCI, a spin table, or a parking mailbox to start each CPU at a given entry point.
start_kernel, setup_arch, and initcallsinit/main.c (start_kernel, do_initcalls, do_one_initcall, initcall_levels, rest_init), include/linux/init.h, include/asm-generic/vmlinux.lds.h (INIT_CALLS), drivers/Makefile, arch/x86/kernel/setup.c, arch/x86/kernel/x86_init.c, arch/arm64/kernel/setup.c (setup_arch), mm/mm_init.c (mm_core_init_early, mm_core_init), mm/memblock.c, arch/x86/kernel/e820.c, arch/x86/kernel/cpu/common.c (arch_cpu_finalize_init), init/calibrate.cThe portable boot. Table A-2's order is the order of calls in start_kernel; setup_arch turns the firmware's memory description into memblock, and since 7.0 mm_core_init_early builds the zones and memory map in one generic place; the initcall levels in init.h, the linker script, and Makefile order decide which subsystem is ready before which.
Hardware discoverydrivers/of/fdt.c (early_init_dt_scan, unflatten_device_tree), drivers/of/platform.c (of_platform_default_populate_init), Documentation/devicetree/bindings/, arch/arm64/kernel/acpi.c (acpi_boot_table_init), drivers/acpi/bus.c (acpi_early_init, acpi_subsystem_init, acpi_init), drivers/acpi/scan.c, include/linux/fwnode.h, include/linux/property.h, drivers/base/dd.c (deferred probe), drivers/base/core.c (fw_devlink)How the device tree and the ACPI tables turn into devices on the driver-model buses of Chapter 13, replacing the fixed-port probing of 2.6-era PC drivers: ARM64's DT-or-ACPI policy and its fall-back when the ACPI tables fail their checks, ACPI's staged bring-up, the firmware-node property API that hides which description a device came from, and the deferred probing and device links that order drivers by their dependencies.
The first processes, the initramfs, and the user-space handoffinit/main.c (rest_init, kernel_init, kernel_init_freeable, do_basic_setup, mark_readonly, run_init_process), init/init_task.c (init_task), include/linux/kernel.h (system_state), kernel/fork.c (user_mode_thread, kernel_thread, fork_idle), kernel/smpboot.c (idle_threads_init), kernel/smp.c (smp_init), kernel/sched/idle.c (do_idle), kernel/kthread.c (kthreadd, kthread_create_list), drivers/base/init.c (driver_init), init/initramfs.c (populate_rootfs), init/do_mounts.c (prepare_namespace), init/do_mounts_initrd.c, usr/default_cpio_list, fs/nullfs.c, fs/namespace.c (init_mount_tree), kernel/exit.c (find_child_reaper), kernel/signal.c (SIGNAL_UNKILLABLE)Where PIDs 0, 1, and 2 come from and why every CPU has its own PID-0 idle task, why PID 1 has been a user_mode_thread rather than a kernel thread since 5.19, how kthreadd serves every kthread_create request, what process 1 does in kernel mode (SMP bring-up, driver_init, the initcalls, freeing and write-protecting init memory), the order in which the kernel looks for an init program (rdinit=, then init=, then the defaults), why the death of PID 1 panics the kernel and how child subreapers share its reaping role, how system_state marks each stage, and how, since Linux 7.0, rootfs sits on an immutable nullfs so both the kernel and early user space can leave the initramfs with an ordinary pivot_root.
The early consoledrivers/tty/serial/earlycon.c (setup_earlycon, param_setup_earlycon), include/linux/serial_core.h (EARLYCON_DECLARE, OF_EARLYCON_DECLARE), drivers/tty/serial/8250/8250_early.c, drivers/firmware/efi/earlycon.c, drivers/acpi/spcr.c (acpi_parse_spcr), arch/x86/kernel/early_printk.c, kernel/printk/printk.c (register_console, CON_BOOT, keep_bootcon, printk_late_init), kernel/printk/nbcon.cHow the kernel prints before the driver model exists: the __earlycon_table linker section and its fixmap-mapped UARTs, the EFI framebuffer console, SPCR and stdout-path discovery for a bare earlycon, x86's older earlyprintk destinations, how boot consoles are retired once a real console registers (or earlier, if their code is in init memory), and why they stay on the synchronous legacy path while real consoles move to printing threads.
kexec and kdumpkernel/kexec.c, kernel/kexec_file.c (kexec_image_verify_sig), kernel/kexec_core.c (kernel_kexec, kexec_load_disabled), kernel/crash_core.c (__crash_kexec, crash hotplug), kernel/crash_reserve.c, kernel/crash_dump_dm_crypt.c, kernel/vmcore_info.c, kernel/elfcorehdr.c, arch/x86/kernel/reboot.c (native_machine_shutdown), arch/x86/kernel/relocate_kernel_64.S, arch/x86/purgatory/purgatory.c, arch/x86/kernel/kexec-bzimage64.c, arch/arm64/kernel/machine_kexec.c, arch/arm64/kernel/relocate_kernel.S, arch/arm64/kernel/kexec_image.c, drivers/of/kexec.c, fs/proc/vmcore.cBooting a kernel from a kernel. The two system calls (only kexec_file_load can enforce signatures, and lockdown forbids kexec_load), the orderly kernel_kexec shutdown versus the panic-time __crash_kexec, ARM64's MMU-on relocation and return to EL2, the x86-only purgatory that checks a SHA-256 digest before the jump, the crashkernel= variants, the elfcorehdr and vmcoreinfo that let tools navigate the dump, and the /proc/vmcore view through which the capture kernel reads the dead one's memory.
Kexec HandOver and Live Updatekernel/liveupdate/kexec_handover.c (kho_preserve_folio, kho_populate, kho_memory_init), kernel/liveupdate/luo_core.c, kernel/liveupdate/luo_session.c, kernel/liveupdate/luo_file.c, mm/memfd_luo.c, include/linux/kho/abi/, Documentation/core-api/kho/index.rstHow a kexec can keep chosen memory: the KHO device tree passed through setup_data or /chosen, the CMA-backed scratch regions that keep the next kernel's early allocations off preserved pages, and the /dev/liveupdate sessions through which user space carries file descriptors (a memfd in 7.0) across a kernel upgrade.
Resuming from hibernationkernel/power/hibernate.c (software_resume, hibernation_restore, hibernation_available), kernel/power/snapshot.c (check_header, get_buffer), kernel/power/swap.c, arch/x86/power/hibernate.c, arch/x86/power/hibernate_64.c, arch/x86/power/hibernate_asm_64.S (core_restore_code), arch/arm64/kernel/hibernate.c (swsusp_arch_resume)A boot that ends by discarding itself: where the boot kernel looks for an image (late initcall or /sys/power/resume), how pages are copied back from a safe page, why x86 can restore with a different kernel while ARM64 demands the identical build, and why lockdown disables hibernation.

Appendix B — Kernel Modules

TopicPrimary filesWhy they matter
From source to .koscripts/Makefile.modpost, scripts/Makefile.modfinal, scripts/mod/modpost.c (add_header, add_depends), scripts/module-common.c, scripts/module.lds.S, scripts/Makefile.modinst, include/linux/module.h (module_init, module_exit), kernel/module/Kconfig (MODULES, MODULE_UNLOAD)How Kbuild and modpost generate foo.mod.c and __this_module, link the .ko, and install it with modules.builtin; and how the same source becomes an initcall when built in.
Linking, relocation, and module placementkernel/module/main.c (simplify_symbols, resolve_symbol_wait, apply_relocations, module_notify_list, free_module), arch/x86/kernel/module.c (apply_relocate_add, module_finalize), arch/x86/include/asm/pgtable_64_types.h (MODULES_VADDR, MODULES_END), arch/arm64/kernel/module.c (module_finalize), arch/arm64/kernel/module-plts.c (module_emit_plt_entry), arch/arm64/mm/init.c (module_direct_base, module_plt_base)How undefined symbols become patched addresses, why relocation ranges pin modules within 2 GB (x86-64) or 128 MB/2 GB (ARM64) of the kernel, the per-architecture code patching applied to each module, and the notifier chain that announces state changes.
The module descriptor and its statesinclude/linux/module.h (struct module, enum module_state, enum mod_mem_type, struct module_memory), kernel/module/internal.h (struct load_info), kernel/module/main.c (elf_validity_cache_index_mod, free_mod_mem)Every field of Tables B-1 and B-2, the four life-cycle states of Figure B-1, the scratch state the loader carries between its stages, and the check that a module’s compiled-in __this_module matches the kernel’s struct module.
Finding and exposing loaded moduleskernel/module/tree_lookup.c (mod_tree_insert, mod_find), kernel/module/main.c (modules, module_mutex, __module_address, modinfo_attrs), kernel/module/procfs.c (m_show), kernel/module/sysfs.cThe global module list, the latched tree that maps an address to its module from any context, and the /proc/modules and /sys/module views built from the descriptor.

Symbol versioningkernel/module/version.c (check_version, check_modstruct_version, module_layout), scripts/mod/modpost.c (add_versions, write_dump), scripts/Makefile.modpost (Module.symvers), scripts/genksyms/, scripts/gendwarfksyms/ (examples/kabi.h), kernel/module/Kconfig (MODVERSIONS, GENDWARFKSYMS, EXTENDED_MODVERSIONS, BASIC_MODVERSIONS)The load-time CRC comparison, how CRCs reach each module and Module.symvers, and the two tools that compute them, including the DWARF-based one and the extended version records that Rust modules require.
Loading and unloadingkernel/module/main.c (init_module and finit_module system calls, copy_module_from_user, idempotent_init_module, load_module, elf_validity_cache_copy, early_mod_check, module_patient_check_exists, add_unformed_module, complete_formation, prepare_coming_module, do_init_module, do_free_init, delete_module, try_stop_module, free_module, try_module_get, module_put, ref_module, strong_try_module_get), include/uapi/linux/module.h (MODULE_INIT_* flags), kernel/module/dups.c (kmod_dup_request_exists_wait), kernel/module/sysfs.c, kernel/module/procfs.c, kernel/module/stats.c, kernel/module/tracking.c, include/linux/fs.h (fops_get), fs/char_dev.c (chrdev_open)The whole path of §4: the system calls, the loader pipeline and its state transitions, reference counting and the owner field, suppression of duplicate loads, the unload refusals and teardown, failed-load statistics and unloaded-taint tracking, and how a live module appears in /sys/module and /proc/modules.
Signing, keyrings, and lockdownkernel/module/signing.c (module_sig_check, mod_verify_sig, is_module_sig_enforced, set_module_sig_enforced), kernel/module_signature.c (mod_check_sig), include/linux/module_signature.h (MODULE_SIG_STRING, struct module_signature), scripts/sign-file.c, scripts/Makefile.modinst (strip, sign, compress), certs/Kconfig (MODULE_SIG_KEY, MODULE_SIG_KEY_TYPE_*, SECONDARY_TRUSTED_KEYRING_SIGNED_BY_BUILTIN), kernel/module/Kconfig (MODULE_SIG_FORCE, MODULE_SIG_ALL, MODULE_SIG_HASH), certs/system_keyring.c (verify_pkcs7_signature), certs/blacklist.c, security/integrity/platform_certs/machine_keyring.c, kernel/params.c (param_set_bool_enable_only, param_check_unsafe), security/lockdown/lockdown.c, security/integrity/ima/ima_efi.c (arch_get_ima_policy)How an appended signature is laid out, found, and verified; which keyrings it is checked against and how keys reach them; why only a missing signature or unknown key is left to policy while a bad one is always fatal; which algorithms (RSA, ECDSA, ML-DSA) and digests a build may choose; and the two ways enforcement is switched on beyond sig_enforce.
Compressionkernel/module/decompress.c (module_decompress, module_decompress_cleanup, compression_show), kernel/module/main.c (init_module_from_file), kernel/module/Kconfig (MODULE_COMPRESS, MODULE_COMPRESS_ALL, MODULE_DECOMPRESS), scripts/Makefile.modinst (cmd_xz)The single build-selected gzip, xz, or zstd decompressor that restores a module to its signed form before load_module runs, its page-by-page output buffer, the second LSM hook on the decompressed bytes, and the install-time compression settings matched to it.
Taintinginclude/linux/panic.h (TAINT_*), kernel/panic.c (taint_flags, print_tainted, print_tainted_verbose, add_taint, proc_taint, panic_on_taint), kernel/module/main.c (module_augment_kernel_taints, module_license_taint_check, module_license_offenders, try_to_force_load, try_force_unload, add_taint_module, module_flags), scripts/mod/modpost.c (intree, staging, test tags), kernel/tracepoint.c (trace_module_has_bad_taint), lib/dump_stack.c (dump_stack_print_info)The taint bits and their oops letters, the build-time tags they are derived from, every point in the loader that raises one, the add-only sysctl and panic_on_taint=, and what a bad taint costs a module’s tracepoints.

Device-table aliases and ueventsinclude/linux/mod_devicetable.h, include/linux/module.h (MODULE_DEVICE_TABLE, MODULE_ALIAS), scripts/mod/file2alias.c (handle_moddevtable, do_pci_entry, do_of_entry), scripts/mod/modpost.c, scripts/depmod.sh, drivers/pci/pci-driver.c (pci_uevent), drivers/base/platform.c (platform_uevent), drivers/of/module.c, drivers/base/core.c (uevent_store), include/linux/cpufeature.h (module_cpu_feature_match)How a driver’s ID table becomes the alias strings that depmod gathers into modules.alias (and, since 6.18, how built-in drivers’ aliases are recorded too); how a bus turns a new device into a MODALIAS uevent that udev matches against those patterns; and how coldplug replays devices found before udev started.
Module parametersinclude/linux/moduleparam.h (module_param, module_param_named, module_param_array, module_param_cb, module_param_unsafe, MODULE_PARM_DESC, KERNEL_PARAM_FL_HWPARAM), kernel/params.c (parse_args, parse_one, param_check_unsafe, kernel_param_lock, module_param_sysfs_setup, param_sysfs_builtin), kernel/module/main.c (unknown_module_param_cb), init/main.c (unknown_bootoption), include/linux/sysfs.h (VERIFY_OCTAL_PERMISSIONS), rust/kernel/module_param.rsHow parameters are declared, typed, and given custom operations; when they are parsed from the load command line or the boot command line (and what happens to unknown ones); how unsafe and hardware parameters are tainted or refused under lockdown; and how they are exposed under /sys/module/<name>/parameters.

Livepatchinclude/linux/livepatch.h (struct klp_patch, struct klp_object, struct klp_func, struct klp_state), include/linux/livepatch_external.h (KLP_RELOC_SEC_PREFIX, KLP_SYM_PREFIX), kernel/livepatch/core.c (klp_enable_patch, klp_apply_section_relocs, klp_apply_object_relocs, klp_module_coming), kernel/livepatch/patch.c (klp_ftrace_handler), kernel/livepatch/transition.c (klp_try_complete_transition, klp_try_switch_task, klp_send_signals, __klp_sched_try_switch), kernel/livepatch/shadow.c (klp_shadow_alloc), kernel/livepatch/state.c (klp_is_patch_compatible), kernel/module/livepatch.c (copy_module_elf), scripts/livepatch/klp-build, tools/objtool/klp-diff.c, Documentation/livepatch/How a patch module is recognized and tainted at load and reaches unexported symbols through KLP relocations; how ftrace redirects each patched function; how the consistency model switches tasks one by one, and what forcing costs; how shadow variables, system states, and cumulative replacement let patches coexist; and how klp-build generates a patch module from a source diff.
